In steady state the tip is usually already final, so the precondition usually holds and the threat is not weakened; the point here is only to state the residual risk accurately (when the tip is not final, this single-block attack must first wait for the tip to become final, which has the same origin as the \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} wait for recovery in \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}). \textbf{The depth upper bound ≤ the length of the unlanded tail}, which lowers the builder-side cost of a fully isolating branch from "a dense coalition" to "one person, one block"; the worst-case depth and the binding constraint that "a lander must collude" both stay unchanged --- this is the price accepted for \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max\_\allowbreak{}landed = ∞}, in exchange for one-hop recovery from a stall of arbitrary length (\hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}/\hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}). "≈ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}" is a conditional upper bound\revnote{77}: the tail length is ≈ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} only if permissionless fallback lands on schedule (the same condition as the \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} liveness bound under a Byzantine aggregator); when fallback stalls, or the landing transaction is censored by L1, the tail grows without bound along with the stall, and a single tier-(ii) block can isolate a tail far longer than \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} --- see that conditional argument in \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} for details. This is not unique to \texttt{∞} (a tier-(i) relay coalition can likewise isolate an over-long tail, merely at greater cost), but \texttt{∞} lowers the cost in that regime to one person and one block, and this must be stated honestly alongside it. The frequency of both tiers is ≤ the number of scheduled slots the coalition obtains, everything is attributable throughout (the parent choice is signed and on record, and the aggregator's truncated landing is public on L1), the only mechanical deterrents are seat value and reputation, and the protocol does not slash builders; but from r41 on, for these isolating branches actually to be finalized they must in addition beat a heavier honest candidate inside the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} settlement window --- as soon as somebody lands the honest tail together with its proof into the window, the isolating branch is self-defeating; the residual risk narrows to "nobody lands the honest tail inside the window" (the fallback feasibility condition, \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}). Complete structural elimination still requires protocol-level DA (Appendix A-3), which is not adopted, per \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}. \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} is thereby a governance knob: tuning it up strengthens gap tolerance (\hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}), tuning it down raises the coalition's scheduling density required for a relayed sparse branch. The direct repacking payoff is still bounded by the capacity of the attacking block itself; the frequency of both kinds of skip is bounded by the fraction of dishonest builders --- the "honest majority" of the \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1} trust model is load-bearing exactly here (this is not conservative redundancy); (3) the harm to users is layered: pure "inclusion"-class commitments are usually honored in the next slot via repacking (the skipper has exactly the incentive to collect the fees of those transactions), while what is genuinely broken are the "ordering / exact state"-class commitments; (4) the handling = public attribution + the registry's liveness/reputation rules (item 1 of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}) + users penalizing repeat offenders with their flow. This is a deterrence-grade residual risk, which this design accepts and states explicitly; structural elimination requires protocol-level DA / availability proofs (for the cost identity see Appendix A-3).
\end{itemize}
\item \textbf{Corollary --- preconfirmation semantics(the version written for user documentation)\revnote{78}:} there are four ways in which the preconfirmation of a signed block can be broken:
\begin{enumerate}
\item The builder that signed it equivocates and a colluding party lands the other fork --- the cost is being slashed directly by L1 for \texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}eq}, priced by the exposure accumulated over the window (\hyperref[sec:4.1]{\S4.1}); the isolable depth is ≤ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} + the fallback response time(a soft upper bound)\revnote{79};
\item Some later builder skips it --- profitable for the skipper (fee/MEV transfer) and not slashable\revnote{80}; publicly visible and fixed by a signature; an adjacent skip affects a single slot, a single-block deep skip affects at most \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max − 1} unlanded blocks, and a minority coalition's relayed sparse branch can reach the entire unlanded tail\revnote{81}; it generally has to win the landing race\revnote{82}--- but no race is needed when the incumbent aggregator colludes\revnote{83}; the frequency is bounded by the honest-majority assumption; pure inclusion-class commitments are usually honored via repacking (\hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4} above);
\item The builder that signed it withholds the block body (\hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}) --- the block can never be landed, the preconfirmation lapses as a matter of course, and the handling is likewise deterrence-grade;
\item \textbf{A deep L1 reorg} --- a residual risk shared by every L2. The correct statement is this: no single participant can annul a batch of preconfirmations "at no cost and silently" --- every path either pays a slashing that L1 can verify directly, or leaves a public record of behavior with a signature on file; but "collusion that can afford the cost" can do it within a bounded depth. This is still stronger than the single seat of v15 (where the silence of one seat annulled the entire epoch's service with nobody able to substitute), but it is not an absolute guarantee and should not be advertised to users as an absolute guarantee. \textbf{The product must distinguish three tiers of commitment\revnote{84}}: (1) inclusion intent --- the transaction will get on chain, and this usually survives skips/withheld bodies (via repacking); (2) ordering/state preconfirmation --- in this design it can be profitably revoked by a single malicious builder in the next slot\revnote{85}, and is backed only by frequency bounds and public attribution, with no slashing; (3) landed finality on L1 --- absolute (up to the L1 reorg depth). User-facing wording must be honestly labeled by tier, and tier 2 must not be advertised as a strong safety commitment.
\end{enumerate}
\end{itemize}
\subsection[Signing the header without publishing the body (withhold-body) --- a known non-slashable misbehavior]{Signing the header without publishing the body (withhold-body) --- a known non-slashable misbehavior\revnote{86}}\label{sec:5.5}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{The behavior}: the builder broadcasts (block header + signature) but withholds the block body. The consequences: later builders cannot compute its state and can only skip it; the aggregator cannot obtain the data and can never land it; the preconfirmations of users holding receipts lapse.
\item \textbf{Structural self-healing\revnote{87}}: a builder can only build on a parent block whose body it holds and whose state it can execute out --- this is a physical fact of execution, and need not be "required" as a rule. A suffix whose bodies are withheld will therefore be automatically bypassed as soon as the first non-colluding builder's turn comes (it has no choice but to attach to the last available block), producing an available fork that anyone can land. For a withheld-body chain to hold up the finality of the whole chain, every scheduled builder after it would have to keep colluding to share the bodies privately --- that is the full-cartel case, governed by the backstop clause of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}.
\item \textbf{The characterization, stated explicitly}: this is not a slashable fault --- "it has the body but does not publish it" is unprovable inside the protocol (the same undecidability as "skipping" has at its root). This design does not pretend to solve it; the handling is:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Preconfirmations before landing explicitly carry a DA assumption}: user documentation must state that "a preconfirmation before landing relies on best-effort availability of the block body on the P2P network, with no protocol-level DA guarantee";
\item The receipt format (\hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}) leaves the victim user holding (block header + signature + inclusion proof) --- public evidence of the withholding behavior, for use by the reputation system and by off-chain accountability;
\item The withholder itself gains nothing (its block earns no fees and is bound to be skipped), so this is purely spiteful behavior, and deterrence rests on reputation and the registry's liveness rules (item 1 of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}).
\end{enumerate}
\item The honest conclusion of this design is therefore\revnote{88}: \textbf{the slashable faults are the two classes that L1 can adjudicate mechanically --- equivocation (\hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3}) and landing-timeout strikes (\hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3})}; a lander landing a worse chain is not a "slashable fault" but "self-defeating"(inside the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window it is overridden by a heavier proven candidate and its cost is spent for nothing --- there is neither a need nor an ability to adjudicate "what ought to be landed" mechanically)\revnote{89}; skipping and withholding bodies at the builder level remain non-slashable. The wording of \hyperref[sec:10]{\S10} and \hyperref[sec:11]{\S11} is updated accordingly. An honest inventory of the weak-enforcement class\revnote{90}: withholding a body usually yields no direct protocol revenue; skipping (deep skipping included) can capture fees/MEV and can revoke ordering/state-class preconfirmations, and is not slashable --- the only handling is frequency bounds (honest majority) and public attribution.
\end{itemize}
\subsection[Settlement windows and heaviest-proven-batch finality (settlement-window finality) --- mechanical, DA-free tail protection ]{Settlement windows and heaviest-proven-batch finality (settlement-window finality) --- mechanical, DA-free tail protection \revnote{91}}\label{sec:5.6}
\begin{designnote}
\textbf{This section supersedes in its entirety the "challenge slashing" of v1.39 (old \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}) and the "chain-head commitment accumulator" of v1.40 (old \hyperref[sec:5.7]{\S5.7}).} Independent review rounds 3 and 4 proved the fatal weakness common to those two layers: for L1 to adjudicate mechanically that "the lander skipped a better chain", L1 must be able to prove that that chain existed at the time, that its block bodies were available, and that its execution was valid --- without full DA plus a challenge/deposit/generational-ledger state machine this simply cannot be done, and doing it would be episode-level complexity flowing back in. On this basis the owner decided to change the root of the design (option C): no longer to adjudicate after the fact that a chain "should have been landed but was not", but instead to relax finality from "the first batch to be proven is final" to "the [heaviest proven batch] inside the settlement window wins and is final". A "better chain" therefore need not be recognized by L1 after the fact --- it lands on its own, carrying its proof, and wins outright inside the window. Comparison takes place only among candidates that have all landed and all passed validity proving, hence: - \textbf{No block-body availability problem} \revnote{92}: a chain that cannot produce its block bodies can never become a candidate at all, and nobody is punished on its account; - \textbf{No commitment-ledger/poisoning problem} \revnote{93}: there is no cross-window commitment state whatsoever; every window starts afresh from the current final head; - \textbf{No challenge/deposit/slashing state machine}: there is no \texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}land}/\texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}chal}/\texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}commit}, no evidence submission, no rebuttal, no response period --- landing a worse chain is not a "punishable fault" but a self-defeating futility (inside the window it is simply overwritten by a heavier candidate, and the gas and proving fees are wasted). Finality is still delivered by the proving system plus a deterministic window --- only batches carrying a validity proof may compete, and window close is a deterministic L1 height; the price is that finality waits one additional \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} (see \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2}; the seconds-scale preconfirmation experience is unchanged).
\end{designnote}

The window lifecycle in a single diagram (in one-to-one correspondence with the pseudocode below and with the executable model of \hyperref[app:C]{Appendix~C}):

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}
  \node[scnode, text width=50mm] (IDLE)
        {No open window\\current final head $= F$};
  \node[scnode, scfill, text width=50mm, below=34mm of IDLE] (OPEN)
        {Window open\\best $=$ heaviest candidate so far\\(provisional, may be superseded)};
  \node[scnode, scfillb, text width=50mm, below=18mm of OPEN] (CLOSE)
        {Close: the one atomic commit\\writes best's end-state tuple\\as the new canonical state\\
         (cursor advances, the tip\\becomes the new final head $F'$)};

  % main transitions (labels on the left)
  \draw[scarr] (IDLE) -- node[sclab, left=1.5mm, text width=44mm]
        {first valid candidate\\extending $F$ lands:\\
         frozen baseline base $=$\\
         $(F,\ \mathrm{stateRoot},$\\
         $F_{\mathrm{consumed}},\ m_{\mathrm{consumed}})$\\
         \texttt{close\_at} $=$ L1 height $+\ W_{\mathrm{settle}}$} (OPEN);
  \draw[scarr] (OPEN) -- node[sclab, left=1.5mm, text width=44mm]
        {L1 height reaches \texttt{close\_at}\\(same height:\\close first, admit after)} (CLOSE);

  % IDLE dashed self-loop (right)
  \draw[scdarr] ($(IDLE.east)+(0,0.22)$) -- ++(0.8,0) |- ($(IDLE.east)+(0,-0.22)$);
  \node[sclab, text width=40mm, anchor=west] at (3.8,0)
        {arriving after the close $\Rightarrow$\\it can only be the first\\candidate of the next window};

  % OPEN self-loop 1
  \draw[scarr] ($(OPEN.east)+(0,0.46)$) -- ++(0.8,0) |- ($(OPEN.east)+(0,0.16)$);
  \node[sclab, text width=40mm, anchor=west] at ($(OPEN.east)+(1.2,0.95)$)
        {heavier valid candidate,\\verified against the same\\frozen base, key strictly\\greater $\Rightarrow$ supersedes best\\(bookkeeping only)};
  % OPEN self-loop 2
  \draw[scarr] ($(OPEN.east)+(0,-0.16)$) -- ++(0.8,0) |- ($(OPEN.east)+(0,-0.46)$);
  \node[sclab, text width=40mm, anchor=west] at ($(OPEN.east)+(1.2,-0.95)$)
        {worse candidate arrives $\Rightarrow$\\revert, zero state change};

  % back to idle after the close
  \draw[scarr] (CLOSE.west) -- ++(-3.4,0) |- (IDLE.west);
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{The life cycle of a settlement window as a state machine: opening the window freezes the baseline, every candidate inside the window is verified against that same baseline and only updates bookkeeping, and not until the close is the heaviest candidate's end-state tuple committed atomically as the new canonical state.}
\label{fig:5}
\end{figure}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Candidates and the settlement window --- including the [baseline freeze + candidate versioning + close commit] state machine \revnote{94}}: let the current window-final head be \texttt{F}. Opening the window immediately freezes the baseline tuple \texttt{base = (\allowbreak{}F,\allowbreak{} F.stateRoot,\allowbreak{} F\_\allowbreak{}consumed,\allowbreak{} m\_\allowbreak{}consumed)}; every candidate inside the window is verified against the same frozen \texttt{base} (the proof's starting cursors/state root = \texttt{base}, consuming the same queue heads), and passing verification records only that candidate's own end tuple \texttt{end\_\allowbreak{}i = (\allowbreak{}tip\_\allowbreak{}i,\allowbreak{} stateRoot\_\allowbreak{}i,\allowbreak{} F\_\allowbreak{}consumed\_\allowbreak{}i,\allowbreak{} m\_\allowbreak{}consumed\_\allowbreak{}i)} together with its \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} weight \texttt{key\_\allowbreak{}i} --- provisional acceptance changes no canonical state (it does not advance cursors, does not switch parents, and does not execute bridge effects). At window close, only the \texttt{end} tuple of the current heaviest candidate (the one with the largest \texttt{key}) is committed atomically, in one step, as the new canonical state; its tip becomes \texttt{F'}, and the next window starts from the new \texttt{base} derived from \texttt{F'}. Pseudocode:
\end{itemize}
\begin{codeblock}
\mbox{}~openWindow(F):~base~←~(F,~F.stateRoot,~F\_consumed,~m\_consumed);~best~←~⊥;~close\_at~←~L1.now~+~W\_settle\\
\mbox{}~acceptCandidate(c):~requires~best=⊥~∨~L1.now~<~close\_at;~\#~close~precedes~acceptance~(r44~normalization,~W2):\\
\mbox{}~\#~at~the~same~L1~height,~closeWindow~is~evaluated~first,~acceptance~afterwards;\\
\mbox{}~\#~a~candidate~arriving~after~that~point~can~only~open~the~next~window\\
\mbox{}~if~best=⊥:~openWindow(current~window-final~head);~\#~the~first~candidate~opens~the~window\\
\mbox{}~verify(c.proof,~from~=~base);~\#~all~candidates~share~one~frozen~baseline\\
\mbox{}~requires~best~=~⊥~∨~key(c)~>~key(best);~\#~§5.2~quadruple~lexicographic~order,~strictly~heavier\\
\mbox{}~best~←~(c.end,~key(c))~\#~bookkeeping~only,~canonical~untouched\\
\mbox{}~closeWindow():~requires~L1.now~≥~close\_at~∧~best~≠~⊥;\\
\mbox{}~canonical~←~best.end;~\#~the~single~atomic~commit\\
\mbox{}~credit(best.beneficiary,\\
\mbox{}~φ\_land~×~Σ~base\_fee(best))~\#~the~base-fee~share~is~booked~at~close~(§6.5,~r47)\\
\end{codeblock}

(What the baseline freeze covers is [the cursors and the state]; it does not include queue contents \revnote{95}: the forced-inclusion/message queues on L1 are append-only, entries are referenced by global sequence number, and an entry's content is immutable once enqueued --- so there is no need to "freeze a queue snapshot": two candidates starting from the same frozen cursor necessarily read the same content at every sequence position; new entries enqueued midway through the window are visible and determinate to any later candidate "whose blocks can reach them" (a heavier candidate legitimately consumes more), and the window state remains a pure function of L1 history --- enqueue events included. In implementation this requires the queue commitment to be organized per-entry and append-only (a hash chain or an MMR), and proofs to reference entries by sequence number; a "single mutable queue root as of the moment of landing" must not be used as a public input --- otherwise the public inputs of successive candidates within a window would drift. Model property P12. \texttt{closeWindow} may be called by anyone, or folded into the first transaction of the next window as a lazy close; the adjudication order at one and the same L1 height is normalized to "close precedes acceptance" --- candidates arriving at or after the \texttt{close\_\allowbreak{}at} height do not take part in this window and can only be the first candidate of the next window, and any implementation (including lazy close) must be consistent with that semantics \revnote{96}; the window state and \texttt{best} are both pure functions of L1 history, and under a shallow L1 reorg they roll back together with L1 --- folded into item 9 of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}.) Stated honestly: this is a small window state machine (three actions: baseline freeze / candidate versioning / close commit) --- far smaller than challenge/deposit/DA or the old episode, but v1.41's claim of "no extra state machine" was inaccurate, and r42 corrects it. The atomic cursor-advance rule of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} is correspondingly re-read: both the starting check and the advance are relative to the window baseline and the close commit, and no longer to "whoever lands first advances".

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Why "the heaviest proven batch" is by itself a mechanical protection of the tail}: an honest lander brings the complete honest tail (necessarily the heaviest under the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} total order --- under an honest majority, any branch that excludes honest blocks contains fewer blocks, see \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}) onto L1 together with its proof, and it wins outright. What if a malicious lander gets ahead by landing a worse chain? Inside the window it is overwritten by the honest candidate; nothing is left on chain and no compensation has to be adjudicated, and the malicious party has paid L1 gas plus proving fees for nothing (a natural griefing cost that requires no design). Every "how do we prove that it should have landed at the time" difficulty of the old v1.39/v1.40 ceases to exist: the chain that ought to land lands by itself and wins, and nobody needs to prove "that it once existed".
\item \textbf{"Sniping" at window close is benign (self-review rounds 1 and 2)}: what if someone lands a slightly heavier candidate at the very last moment before close? Under the total order, "heavier = more genuine signed blocks in the same lane" --- forgery is impossible (forging would require stealing a builder's private key), so a heavier candidate necessarily contains more genuine preconfirmed blocks, and its winning is better for users (a more complete tail is finalized). The only party harmed is the earlier lander, in gas --- an economic residual risk, not a safety residual risk. A fixed window therefore suffices, with no need for a chess clock or extensions (an extension would instead introduce gameable timing). Withholding a block body and releasing it late (a builder withholds the body → the fallback lands the visible tail → before close the attacker releases the body, lands the full tail and overwrites it) is the same story: the result is that a more complete tail is finalized, and the fallback lander that was "overwritten" loses gas --- handled through the fallback-compensation path of \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} and listed as an economic residual risk.
\item \textbf{The setter invariant for \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle}}: \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle ≥ P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} margin} --- this guarantees that, from window opening onward, anyone who sees a heavier tail has time to prove it and land it. An honest lander need not wait for the window: it starts proving as soon as it sees the tail, so under normal conditions the heaviest candidate is already on its way when the window opens. The steady-state cost is zero: with no competition the window contains only the aggregator's own single candidate, and nobody spends an extra cent --- the competition mechanism is exercised only when the system is under attack.
\item \textbf{The window state is a pure function of L1 history}: candidates are all L1 transactions, window opening and close are L1 heights, and the current best is the deterministic result of transaction-by-transaction comparison --- any node that replays L1 obtains the same window state; under a shallow L1 reorg it rolls back together with L1 (folded into the atomic-rollback list of item 9 of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}, with no extra state machine).
\item \textbf{Liveness and anti-spam (self-review rounds 5 and 7)}: superseding requires a strictly greater weight plus a valid proof, so a candidate spammer must pay a real proving fee every time and is capped by the length of the real tail --- there is no livelock; a candidate arriving after window close is benignly stranded (it targets the old \texttt{F}, and it suffices to rebuild it onto the new \texttt{F'}, the same stranding semantics as in \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}). The candidate's beneficiary (the reward/refund address) is built into the proof's public inputs, so mempool copying cannot change where it points \revnote{97}.
\item \textbf{Explicitly stated residual risks (stated honestly)}: (a) temporary ordering swings during the window: the "current best candidate" inside a window may be superseded by a heavier one, so the temporary head that L2 nodes follow is mutable within the window --- this corresponds precisely to the existing tier-2 preconfirmation semantics (revocable, decided by window close); it adds no new exposure and merely makes that exposure explicit; withdrawals and bridging execute only from window-final state (\hyperref[sec:6.1]{\S6.1}/\hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2}). (b) \textbf{Fallback for excluded blocks}: if the heaviest candidate still excludes certain genuine blocks (for example, an attacker wins some window with a sparse branch built on enough slots of its own in the lookahead --- under an honest majority this requires a coalition with dense lookahead scheduling, bounded as in \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}), the excluded transactions return to the mempool/forced-inclusion queue as before and are repacked in the next window --- the worst-case depth is still ≤ the unlanded tail, the same bound as the existing residual risk of \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}, and C does not widen it. (c) \textbf{Finality + \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle}}: this is the real cost of option C, and the owner accepts it explicitly (accounted for in \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2}).
\end{itemize}
\section[Landing]{Landing}\label{sec:6}
\subsection[Batches]{Batches}\label{sec:6.1}
\begin{itemize}
\item A batch = a contiguous segment of the signature chain \texttt{[m+\allowbreak{}1..k]} (extending from the current window-final head \texttt{m}, \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}) + block-body data (blob) + a validity proof that verifies all the rules of \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} together with the per-block state transitions.
\item \textbf{Atomic landing + window finality \revnote{98}}: data, proof and state root are still submitted in a single transaction and verified on the spot by L1 --- there is still no "proposed but not yet proven" intermediate state, no sealing deadline / cancellation horizon / cancellation cascade / timing-exemption net (the v15 complexity reduction is retained). But what "verification passed" now yields is the current best candidate (provisional) of that window, rather than immediate finality; finality is granted at settlement-window close to the heaviest proven candidate in the window (\hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}). Under normal conditions, with no competition, the aggregator's candidate is the only candidate and close is finality --- the semantic difference appears only under attack. \textbf{Withdrawal and L2→L1 bridging effects execute only from window-final state}, never from provisional state.
\item Batch size is bounded above by blob capacity and proving cost; batches need not align with epoch boundaries.
\item \textbf{Future slots must not be landed \revnote{99}}: the \texttt{slot} of a batch's last block must be ≤ the \texttt{L2\_\allowbreak{}slot} corresponding to the timestamp of the L1 block in which it lands (the permitted clock skew is initially 0) --- checked at the L1 entry point and proven identically in the circuit. The lookahead is published 768 slots in advance; without this constraint a builder could pre-sign blocks for future slots, and a batch could push the landed chain head ahead of wall-clock time --- destroying the timestamp/anchoring/forced-expiry semantics and artificially driving the \texttt{lag} of \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} negative. Correspondingly, the arithmetic definition of \texttt{lag} is truncated below at 0 (safe arithmetic).
\end{itemize}
\subsection[Cadence]{Cadence}\label{sec:6.2}
\begin{itemize}
\item Target landing cadence: the aggregator should keep the lag \texttt{lag} defined in \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} (the number of slots by which the tip of the best provisional candidate trails L1 wall-clock time --- observable on L1, referring to no P2P state)\revnote{100} within \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}. A proving latency of about 10–15 minutes means that in steady state provisional landing trails the live chain head by about 15–20 minutes; window-final finality adds one further \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} (≈ one round of proving plus landing, \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}/\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12})\revnote{101} --- the decoupling of the preconfirmation experience (seconds-scale) from the finality cadence (minutes-scale) is unchanged, and it is the former that users perceive. The ladder of four levels of certainty, and the two lags:
\end{itemize}
\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}
  \node[scnode, text width=56mm] (P1)
        {Levels 1 and 2: preconfirmation\\builder signature, seconds};
  \node[scnode, text width=56mm, below=14mm of P1] (P2)
        {provisional landing\\the candidate is on L1,\\a heavier one may still supersede it\\within the window\\
         steady state $\mathrm{lag}_{\mathrm{prov}} \approx 15$--$20$ min};
  \node[scnode, scfill, text width=56mm, below=14mm of P2] (P3)
        {window-final\\withdrawals and bridging\\execute from here\\
         steady state $\mathrm{lag}_{\mathrm{final}} \approx 35$--$40$ min};
  \node[scnode, scfillb, text width=56mm, below=14mm of P3] (P4)
        {L1-final\\absolute finality};

  \draw[scarr] (P1) -- (P2);
  \draw[scarr] (P2) -- node[sclab, left=1.5mm, text width=26mm]
        {$+\ W_{\mathrm{settle}}$\\$\approx 20$ min} (P3);
  \draw[scarr] (P3) -- node[sclab, left=1.5mm, text width=26mm]
        {$+\ F_{\mathrm{l1}}$\\(minutes)} (P4);

  \node[scdnote, text width=48mm, anchor=west] (N1) at ($(P2.east)+(0.7,0)$)
        {service-observation threshold\\$\Delta_{\mathrm{lag,prov}} = 4$ epoch $\approx 25.6$ min};
  \node[scdnote, text width=48mm, anchor=west] (N2) at ($(P3.east)+(0.7,0)$)
        {fallback / strike threshold\\$\Delta_{\mathrm{lag,final}} \approx 9$ epoch $\approx 57.6$ min\\
         $=\Delta_{\mathrm{lag,prov}} + W_{\mathrm{settle,max}} +$ margin};

  \draw[scdarr] (P2.east) -- (P2.east -| N1.west);
  \draw[scdarr] (P3.east) -- (P3.east -| N2.west);
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{The four-level certainty ladder and the two lags: preconfirmation is a matter of seconds, and provisional landing is one settlement window away from window-final. $\mathrm{lag}_{\mathrm{prov}}$ serves only to observe the service tempo, while it is $\mathrm{lag}_{\mathrm{final}}$ that enters fallback and slashing accounting.}
\label{fig:6}
\end{figure}

\begin{itemize}
\item L2→L1 bridge (withdrawal) delay = landing cadence + \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} (withdrawals execute only from window-final state, \hyperref[sec:6.1]{\S6.1}).
\item \textbf{No fast close (fast path)}: even when a window contains only a single candidate, the full \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} is awaited before finality --- a test for "closing early" would reintroduce gameable timing, and v1 does not do it; it is listed in \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} as an optimization (for example, "a candidate covering all known signature-chain heads may close early" may be introduced only after it has been proven not to be manipulable by block withholding).
\item \textbf{Two lags, each with its own role \revnote{102}}: \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}prov} = the amount by which the tip of the best provisional candidate trails --- used only for observing service cadence (governance signals such as whether the aggregator ought to be replaced); \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final} = the amount by which the window-final head trails --- used for safety/revocable- exposure accounting and for fallback accounting (the next item and \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}). Two thresholds, in one-to-one correspondence \revnote{103}: \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov} (= the old \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}, initially 4 epoch ≈ 25.6 min, setter invariant \texttt{≥ the upper bound of the normal provisional lag band})\revnote{104} applies to \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}prov}; \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final = Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov +\allowbreak{} W\_\allowbreak{}settle\_\allowbreak{}max(\allowbreak{}wall-\allowbreak{}clock conversion) +\allowbreak{} margin} (initially ≈ 9 epoch ≈ 57.6 min)\revnote{105} applies to \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final}. Rationale: under window finality the steady-state \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final ≈ lag\_\allowbreak{}prov +\allowbreak{} W\_\allowbreak{}settle ≈ 35–40 min}, which by itself already exceeds the old threshold of 25.6 min --- if fallback/strikes were adjudicated on \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final} while the old threshold were retained, the fallback window would be permanently open in a completely healthy system, the "cost + markup" reward commitment would remain in force in perpetuity, and a diligent aggregator would accrue strikes continuously until termination; r42's switching the adjudicated quantity without raising the threshold is precisely this bug. The honest bound on revocable exposure (medium 1): although a provisional candidate has already landed on L1, it can still be superseded before close, so the worst-case revocable set of tier-2 preconfirmation = the unlanded tail + the provisional increment within the window, and the true bound = \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov} + \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} (wall clock) + fallback response ≈ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final} + fallback response --- it can no longer be written as "≈ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}" on its own; elsewhere in this document, the \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} appearing in depth bounds of the form "depth ≈ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} + fallback response" is, per r44, to be read as the fallback-window-opening threshold \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final} (a new name for the same quantity; the bound is unchanged, only the naming is made honest). In order to pin this bound down, \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} is given, besides its lower bound, a consensus upper bound \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle\_\allowbreak{}max} (\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}; including the margin for wall-clock conversion when L1 slots are missed), which is folded into the user-facing semantics and the wording of \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}.
\end{itemize}
\subsection[Landing Obligation and Permissionless Fallback (This Is the Aggregator's Only Obligation)]{Landing Obligation and Permissionless Fallback (This Is the Aggregator's Only Obligation)}\label{sec:6.3}
\begin{designnote}
\textbf{This section was rewritten after review r1-2.} The v1 draft stated the obligation as "cover up to the P2P chain head" --- but the P2P chain head is a quantity that is not observable on L1, so the obligation cannot be enforced mechanically; a malicious aggregator that lands a micro-batch containing only 1 block just before every deadline could both "never time out" and let finality fall arbitrarily far behind. The fix: move every determination onto the L1-observable \textbf{lag}, and make the fallback landing itself the incriminating evidence of the aggregator's dereliction.
\end{designnote}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Lag (L1-observable)}: \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}* = L2 slot number of the current L1 timestamp − slot number of the reference head}; taking the tip of the best provisional candidate as the reference head yields \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}prov}, and taking the window-final head yields \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final} (the \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2} split)\revnote{106}. Terminological convention \revnote{107}: in the remainder of this section (window opening / strikes / late fees / threshold-riding determinations), bare \texttt{lag}/\texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} are always to be read as \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final}/\texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final}; \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}prov}/ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov} carry only the service-level observation of \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2} and enter no penalty rule; the \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} appearing in historical calibration notes (r10-2 and others) refers to the single threshold of that time, whose value is now inherited by \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov}. Normative definition \revnote{108}: \texttt{L2\_\allowbreak{}slot(\allowbreak{}t) = floor(\allowbreak{}(\allowbreak{}t − GENESIS\_\allowbreak{}L2) /\allowbreak{} 1 second)}, where \texttt{t} is the timestamp of the L1 execution block in which the determination is made (constrained by L1 consensus, a single proposer has only second-scale jitter influence); it references no L2 or P2P data. Both quantities are pure functions of L1 state, and any contract call can compute them.
\item The causal chain of the fallback window and the strikes (the precise definition of each link is given in the subsequent items of this section):
\end{itemize}
\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}
  \node[scnode, text width=62mm] (W)
        {Observe $\mathrm{lag}_{\mathrm{final}}$\\(a pure function of L1 state)};
  \node[scdec, text width=28mm, below=10mm of W] (C)
        {$\mathrm{lag}_{\mathrm{final}} > \Delta_{\mathrm{lag,final}}$ ?};
  \node[scsm, text width=38mm, right=10mm of C] (N)
        {Fallback window closed\\normal case: only\\the aggregator lands};
  \node[scnode, scfill, text width=62mm, below=14mm of C] (O)
        {Fallback window open\\(a state condition, not a timer)\\
         eligibility and reward commitment are\\\emph{snapshotted} until the window closes};
  \node[scnode, text width=62mm, below=8mm of O] (A)
        {Anyone may land a valid candidate\\
         reward $=$ cost $+$ premium,\\paid out of the aggregator's bond};
  \node[scdec, text width=30mm, below=14mm of A] (S)
        {who supplied the batch\\accepted while open?};
  \node[scsm, text width=40mm] (K1) at ($(S.south)+(-4.9,-1.6)$)
        {fallback strike\\(rate-limited by $G_{\mathrm{strike}}$)};
  \node[scsm, text width=40mm] (K2) at ($(S.south)+(4.9,-1.6)$)
        {late fee $+$ late count\\(each rate-limited)};
  \node[scdec, text width=32mm] (T) at ($(S.south)+(0,-4.2)$)
        {reached $m_{\mathrm{agg}} = 2$\\or $m'_{\mathrm{agg}} = 4$ ?};
  \node[scnode, scfillb, text width=62mm, below=8mm of T] (E)
        {seat terminated $\Rightarrow$ top standby promoted};

  \draw[scarr] (W) -- (C);
  \draw[scarr] (C) -- node[sclab, above=0.4mm] {no} (N);
  \draw[scarr] (C) -- node[sclab, left=1mm] {yes} (O);
  \draw[scarr] (O) -- (A);
  \draw[scarr] (A) -- (S);
  \draw[scarr] (S.west) -- node[sclab, midway] {not the aggregator} (K1.north);
  \draw[scarr] (S.east) -- node[sclab, midway] {the aggregator itself (late)} (K2.north);
  \draw[scarr] (K1.south) -- (T.west);
  \draw[scarr] (K2.south) -- (T.east);
  \draw[scarr] (T) -- node[sclab, left=1mm] {yes} (E);
  \draw[scarr] (T.west) -- ++(-5.0,0)
        node[sclab, above=0.4mm, pos=0.75] {no} |- (W.west);
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{The causal chain of the permissionless fallback window and of seat strikes: fallback eligibility is opened by a state condition, namely $\mathrm{lag}_{\mathrm{final}}$ crossing its threshold as observed on L1. Who lands while the window is open decides what is recorded: a fallback strike, accrued at a rate limited by $G_{\mathrm{strike}}$, or a late fee priced on the overshoot together with a separately rate-limited late count. Strikes accumulating to the threshold terminate the seat.}
\label{fig:7}
\end{figure}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{The fallback window is a state condition, not a timer}: whenever \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final > Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final} (initially ≈ 9 epochs ≈ 57.6 minutes = \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov +\allowbreak{} W\_\allowbreak{}settle\_\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} margin})\revnote{109}, the fallback window is automatically in the open state --- anyone may land any valid batch and be paid from the aggregator's bond at indexed cost (L1 fees + proving cost + markup) (a transplant of the fault-pays model of v15 \hyperref[sec:6.7]{\S6.7}). The window does not close because "the aggregator landed a batch" --- it closes only because \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final} falls back within \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final}. Micro-batches are therefore ineffective: landing 1 block does not reduce the lag, and the window stays open regardless. Fallback eligibility and reward are determined by \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final} and are snapshot-held until the settlement window closes \revnote{110}: once the fallback window has opened under \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final > Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final}, the eligibility and the "cost + markup" reward commitment remain valid for every candidate within this settlement window until close; the reward is paid to the closing winner and to every candidate that strictly improved the best chain (covering the full proving + inclusion cost) --- an honest improver overtaken by a heavier candidate does not work for nothing, and a short provisional candidate cannot take the eligibility away.
\item \textbf{Fault and incriminating evidence (mechanically decided on L1)}: one "strike" = a batch from someone other than the aggregator was accepted while the fallback window was open. The elegance of this definition lies in its zero false positives: if there is simply no block available to land on P2P (all builders absent), then nobody can land anything and the aggregator is not struck for "having nothing to land"; whereas once someone does land, that very fact proves on L1 that "there was landable content and the aggregator did not land it" --- the dereliction is self-evidencing by fact, with no need to observe P2P. Each strike slashes one tier of the aggregator's liveness bond. Strikes accumulate with the duration for which the window stays open and are rate-limited by \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike} \revnote{111}: the unit of a strike is changed to a sustained open-window interval --- for each full \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike} (initially 1 epoch) that an open-window period (a contiguous interval in which \texttt{lag > Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}) persists, one strike is counted if ≥ 1 non-aggregator batch was accepted within that interval. Two properties are preserved simultaneously: (i) rate limiting against spam: however many micro-batches an attacker splits its landings into within the same \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike} interval, only one strike is counted, and the attacker cannot accelerate time \revnote{112}; (ii) sustained dereliction necessarily accumulates: as long as the window stays open and fallback landings keep occurring, the strike count rises once per \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike}, so \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg} (2 strikes) is reached within ≈ 2 epochs and the aggregator is replaced --- the drip-feed stalling of r13-1 falls precisely into this branch. The first strike still requires the window to have been continuously open for ≥ \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike} (the grace period for transient faults is unchanged). The symmetric reverse weaponization (a malicious fallback lander drip-feeds micro-batches to invalidate the aggregator's catch-up proofs and to run up strikes on its behalf) is met as follows: the aggregator's own batches never incur a strike and it may bid arbitrarily high to get in first, and batch proofs must support incremental continuation/aggregation from an already-proved prefix (an engineering requirement, folded into \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 6) --- once a micro-batch that advances the chain head no longer forces anyone to re-prove from scratch, the drip-feed's teeth are pulled; the residual risk is still "sustained L1-level censorship of the aggregator's landing transactions" (the most expensive censorship category, already priced in v15 \hyperref[sec:11.5]{\S11.5}), and is explicitly accepted. Bond exhaustion is itself termination: late fees and strikes draining the liveness bond through the floor (below the minimum maintenance amount, a calibration item) counts as a termination trigger, so the fees are also an independent termination path and no longer mere bleeding.
\item \textbf{A late landing is itself charged --- closing the "threshold-riding" strategy \revnote{113}}: counting strikes solely on "a fallback batch was accepted" has one hole: an online malicious aggregator can squat right at the threshold --- as soon as \texttt{lag} just exceeds \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} and the fallback window opens, it front-runs the fallback lander with its own catch-up batch, closes the window, never takes a strike, keeps finality permanently riding the \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} line, and burns the fallback lander's proving cost for nothing. The fix (equally mechanically decidable on L1): any batch accepted while \texttt{lag > Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} --- including the aggregator's own --- charges the aggregator a late fee priced by the excess (deducted from the liveness bond), and the event of the aggregator's own late batch being accepted is recorded in a lighter violation counter, which upon reaching \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg'} (initially 4) likewise triggers termination. The counter is attached to "a late batch was accepted" rather than to "the window was opened" \revnote{114}: the window may open for reasons unrelated to the aggregator, such as all builders being absent or a proving-system failure, and in that case nobody can land at all, so any count would be unjust; whereas "a late batch was accepted" (whether the aggregator's own or a fallback lander's) itself proves on L1 that "there was landable content", so zero false positives holds. The threshold-riding front-running loop remains closed: every time the front-runner closes the window it necessarily lands a late batch of its own, and every such time is counted. Honest degradation \revnote{115}: "not a penny is charged during an externality-caused failure" currently holds fully only for the fallback strike; the late fee and the late counter can still hit "the honest aggregator that lands the first recovery batch after a network-wide proving-system failure is resolved" (such a recovery batch is necessarily late). Fix one: the late counter accumulates under the same \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike} rate limit as the fallback strike (for each \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike} of sustained window opening within whose interval one of the aggregator's own late batches was accepted, at most one is counted)\revnote{116}. Fix two: the exemption mechanism for systemic proving outages is elevated to a blocking open item (\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 8) --- until it is fully defined and mechanically verifiable, the "zero false positives" claim is limited to the fallback strike and does not cover the late fee. The landing gas lost by a front-run fallback lander is compensated out of the late fee according to a pre-registered intent (the detailed rules are folded into \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 3).
\item \textbf{Closing the zero-penalty reset of "self-wiping the lag + landing a worse chain" \revnote{117}}: charging solely on "whether \texttt{lag} is \texttt{> Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} at acceptance time" gives the aggregator a threshold-pinning loop: it can time its landing exactly at \texttt{lag = Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} (not \texttt{>}) and land a forced-only/short chain of its own that overrides the healthy content tail; acceptance then carries a zero late fee, the lag drops back after landing, and the cycle repeats every proving period --- finality is suppressed indefinitely at zero penalty and the seat is never replaced. Two closures: (a) \textbf{Landing a worse chain is self-defeating (the main closure)\revnote{118}}: each worse forced-only/short chain it lands is merely one candidate inside the window --- anyone can land that healthy content tail with a proof into the same window, and by the total order it directly overrides the malicious candidate; the malicious candidate wastes gas + proving fees and suppresses nothing. "Zero penalty, repeatable" becomes "zero revenue, guaranteed loss", and no evidence submission or slashing adjudication is required. (b) \textbf{The lag debt is settled against the pre-acceptance state, leaving no room for integer threshold-riding \revnote{119}}: the determination for the late fee and the late counter is changed to \texttt{lag ≥ Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}, and the fee is priced as \texttt{late\_\allowbreak{}units = max(\allowbreak{}0,\allowbreak{} preLag − (\allowbreak{}Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag − 1))} --- that is, at \texttt{lag = Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} we have \texttt{late\_\allowbreak{}units = 1} (the minimum non-zero late fee, no longer an "excess = 0 exemption point"); the \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg'} counter is changed to maintain independently, for "one of the aggregator's own late batches was accepted", a rate-limit state keyed on the L1 slot of the last count (no longer depending on the old condition "the window has been continuously open for a full \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike}", which is undefined at the equality point --- finding 8 pointed out that the old rate-limit definition has neither an open window nor any duration at \texttt{= Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}). Timing a landing precisely at \texttt{= Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} is thereby no longer penalty-free and no longer strike-free. Rate-limit units are unified \revnote{120}: the normative unit of \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike} is L2 seconds (1 epoch = 384 L2 seconds); the \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg'} rate-limit state stores "the L1 slot of the last count", and the comparison converts via \texttt{(\allowbreak{}current L1 slot − last L1 slot) × 12 ≥ G\_\allowbreak{}strike\_\allowbreak{}L2s} --- eliminating the ambiguity in which "a count of L1 slots is used directly as L2 epochs", which causes a factor-of-12 discrepancy. The two act together: the self-wiping-lag loop either lands the genuinely better tail (harmless) or is overridden inside the window at a pure loss plus a late fee, and the "Byzantine aggregator is bounded" claim of \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}/\hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} is restored (under the same fallback-feasibility condition that someone within the window is willing to land the honest tail).
\item \textbf{Termination and replacement}: an accumulated \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg} fallback strikes (initially 2) or \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg'} of the aggregator's own late batches (initially 4) → the seat is terminated and the highest standby is promoted, with a promotion delay of 1 epoch (in this design the aggregator does not issue preconfirmations, so a replacement is not perceptible at the service level and the delay can be aggressive).
\item \textbf{Reward anti-grinding}: the aggregator's service reward is charged by the number of slots that a landing advances, not by the number of batches --- a micro-batch not only fails to suppress the fallback window, it also earns nothing.
\item \textbf{Aggregator liveness is a "bound under an economic assumption", not a pure protocol bound \revnote{121}}: strikes and \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg'} accumulate only when a batch is accepted inside the fallback window. If the aggregator silently stops landing and nobody performs a fallback landing --- because the proving cost exceeds the reward, because of an L1 fee spike, or because no prover is available at all --- then no strike is produced, the seat does not change hands, and finality may stall indefinitely. The bound on the "Byzantine aggregator is bounded" row of the \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} table is therefore conditional: it holds if and only if permissionless fallback is economically feasible (someone is willing and able to bear the proving and gas cost of the fallback). This is an economic/engineering assumption, not protocol-enforced liveness; stating it explicitly is an honesty clause of the same kind as \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}'s "honest majority is an economic assumption". The indexed-cost cap on the fallback reward and the fallback incentive under extreme congestion are listed as calibration items in \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} items 3 and 7, and the availability/cost of fallback provers is formally folded into the \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1} threat model and \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}.
\item \textbf{The availability precondition for fallback \revnote{122}}: a fallback landing requires the block bodies in hand, and before landing, block-body availability is only best-effort guaranteed by P2P (\hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}). The exact condition for "the Byzantine aggregator is bounded" is therefore: the unlanded tail, or some available fork of it, is available to at least one lander willing to perform a fallback. By the structural self-healing of \hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}, this reduces to "there exists ≥ 1 non-colluding builder in the lookahead" (which automatically routes around the segment whose bodies are withheld and produces a fork that anyone can land) --- that precondition is directly implied by the \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1} trust model (an honest majority of builders) with ample margin, so within the model the boundedness of a Byzantine aggregator is unconditional. If the builders and the aggregator collude as a whole (bodies kept private, nobody able to land anything), the strike mechanism indeed does not fire --- which is the correct behavior of the zero-false-positive principle (the stall is then attributable to the cartel, not to the individual aggregator); by the \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1} trust model that case is out-of-model, the protocol offers it no finality guarantee, and only the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} forced path is retained as defense in depth: a user submits a forced entry → the deadline passes and the lag exceeds its limit → anyone advances finality with a forced-only block.
\item \textbf{Key property}: because landing requires no authority (\hyperref[sec:0]{\S0} item 3), the fallback requires no new trust. Aggregator offline: the sequencing service is entirely unaffected (builders keep producing blocks and preconfirmations keep being issued), and finality is maintained near \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} by fallback landers. Aggregator online but maliciously stalling (Byzantine): micro-batches cannot save it --- once the lag exceeds the limit the fallback opens, once a fallback landing occurs a strike is counted, and after \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg} strikes the aggregator is replaced; under the availability precondition of the previous item, the worst-case finality lag is pinned at \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} + the fallback response time, and is bounded. These two cases are listed separately from the full-cartel case in the table of \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}.
\end{itemize}
\subsection[Stall and Gap Recovery --- Ordinary Block Production Takes Over in One Hop via the Landing Head, with No Episode (v1.31, Direction B Redesign)]{Stall and Gap Recovery --- Ordinary Block Production Takes Over in One Hop via the Landing Head, with No Episode (v1.31, Direction B Redesign)}\label{sec:6.4}
\begin{designnote}
\textbf{This section wholly replaces the "re-anchor episode" of v1.8–v1.30} (announce / existence challenge / deposits \texttt{B\_\allowbreak{}ra}, \texttt{B\_\allowbreak{}ch} / freshness window / \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}base}, \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}ra} / \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}cont} continuation / re-pinning / state machine). The foundation of that mechanism was "pinning a block inside a freshness window of wall-clock slots", whereas proving latency (10–15 minutes) and L1 landing delay are >> the slot / \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} (64 seconds) timing window, which makes any timing rule of that kind inherently fragile; across roughly eight rounds of review (r23 → r24 → r25 → r28 + round 7 P1 / deposit determinism r29-P2) it repeatedly produced second-order holes with the same root, and point patches only relocated them without converging. On this basis the owner decided (2026-08-25, Direction B) on a wholesale redesign: \textbf{recovery is no longer a separate sub-protocol but a direct corollary of the tier (ii) parent rule of \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}.} This redesign has undergone six rounds of adversarial self-review by the designer (see the v1.31 entry of \hyperref[app:D]{Appendix~D}).
\end{designnote}

After a stall of hours or even days, recovery is merely "one landing + one tier (ii) block":

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[font=\scriptsize]
  \def\pL{0}\def\pO{4.0}\def\pB{8.0}\def\pP{11.6}

  % participants
  \node[scnote, text width=24mm, anchor=north] at (\pL,0) {Lander\\(aggregator\\or anyone)};
  \node[scnote, text width=24mm, anchor=north] at (\pO,0) {L1 contract};
  \node[scnote, text width=24mm, anchor=north] at (\pB,0) {Any returning\\scheduled builder};
  \node[scnote, text width=24mm, anchor=north] at (\pP,0) {P2P network};

  % lifelines
  \foreach \x in {\pL,\pO,\pB,\pP}{\draw[sclife] (\x,-1.3) -- (\x,-12.2);}

  % Note over Lander..P2P
  \node[scnote, text width=110mm] at (5.8,-2.0)
        {A stall happens: the gap exceeds $G_{\max}$,\\the wall clock runs far ahead of the chain head};

  % (1)
  \draw[scarr] (\pL,-4.1) -- (\pO,-4.1);
  \node[sclab, text width=48mm, anchor=south] at (2.0,-4.02)
        {(1) first land the best pre-stall tail\\(frozen long tail included),\\as a candidate with its proof};

  % (2) L1 self-message (dashed)
  \draw[scdarr] (\pO,-5.1) -- ++(0.85,0) -- ++(0,-0.55) -- (\pO,-5.65);
  \node[sclab, text width=48mm, anchor=west] at (\pO+1.05,-5.38)
        {(2) the settlement window closes $\Rightarrow$\\the tail is window-final, final head $=F$};

  % (3) builder self-message
  \draw[scarr] (\pB,-6.9) -- ++(0.85,0) -- ++(0,-0.55) -- (\pB,-7.45);
  \node[sclab, text width=38mm, anchor=west] at (\pB+1.05,-7.18)
        {(3) wait for $F$'s landing tx\\to reach $F_{\mathrm{l1}}$ depth (minutes)};

  % (4)
  \draw[scarr] (\pB,-9.3) -- (\pP,-9.3);
  \node[sclab, text width=60mm, anchor=south] at (9.8,-9.22)
        {(4) sign a tier (ii) recovery block\\on parent $F$ (\texttt{final\_ref} witnesses\\that the parent is final)};

  % Note over builder..P2P
  \node[scnote, text width=62mm] at (9.8,-10.3)
        {one hop back to the wall clock; from here on,\\normal tier (i) blocks, whatever the stall lasted};

  % (5)
  \draw[scarr] (\pL,-11.9) -- (\pO,-11.9);
  \node[sclab, text width=58mm, anchor=south] at (2.0,-11.82)
        {(5) the recovery batch is proven, landed\\and opens a new window as usual};
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{Recovery after a stall of hours or even days: first land the best pre-stall tail and let it close into the final head $F$, then let any returning builder sign a tier (ii) recovery block on parent $F$, which reattaches the ordering to the wall clock in a single hop. Recovery involves no separate sub-protocol.}
\label{fig:8}
\end{figure}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{The mechanism (just one rule)}: when the gap > \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} (consecutive absences exceed the cap), or when the aggregator withholds landing so that normal block production cannot continue on the unlanded tail --- any lookahead builder of the current turn takes the current final L1 landing head as parent and produces an ordinary block under tier (ii) of \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} (a content block; under a full cartel, a forced-only block from any key, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}). That block's \texttt{slot} = the wall-clock slot of its turn, and the parent distance is arbitrary (tier (ii) has no cap). L2 sequencing thereby recovers: the landing head is reconnected to near the wall clock, and subsequent builders produce blocks on top of it normally (tier (i)). The production/landing of the recovery block itself has no announcement, deposit, freshness window, or state machine --- it is merely an ordinary candidate in the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} settlement window, competing with the other candidates by the total order. The ordering rule of landing the best chain first is given in the next item: if a frozen long tail exists from before the stall, land it first and recover from its tip, so that its preconfirmations are not lost.
\item \textbf{Land the best (including frozen) chain first, then recover from its final tip --- do not discard the tail \revnote{123}}: v1.35 once stated that "the frozen long tail from before the stall yields to a single recovery block", which is wrong --- it would instruct honest landers to discard a landable healthy long tail. The correct order is: (1) by the total order of \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} the pre-stall long tail is still the best chain ("cannot be extended over P2P" ≠ "should be discarded"), so a lander lands it first (honoring all of its preconfirmations; the \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} gap merely prevents P2P from extending it further and does not prevent proving and posting it); (2) once it has landed and reached \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} it becomes the new final tip, and the recovery block is produced with that new tip as parent under tier (ii) of \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}, reconnecting to the wall clock in one hop from there. The "recovery vs. fork-choice deadlock" thus disappears (not by discarding the tail, but by landing the tail first), and the preconfirmations of the healthy long tail are not sacrificed. Only when there genuinely is no longer/better chain (a true total stop, with not even a frozen tail) is the recovery block itself the best chain and landed directly. Skipping a better tail in order to land a recovery block is self-defeating: anyone can land the better tail into the same \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window and it wins \revnote{124}.
\item \textbf{"One hop" means re-rooting; sustained progress still requires the normal liveness floor \revnote{125}}: the recovery block \texttt{R} (tier (ii)) re-roots sequencing onto the landing head in one hop; but \texttt{R} is itself an unlanded P2P block, and to genuinely reconnect to the wall clock a subsequent builder must extend it within \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} (tier (i), exactly as for every normal block) --- proving and landing \texttt{R} takes 10–15 minutes, and during that time sequencing continues over P2P on top of \texttt{R} (the normal "preconfirmation in seconds, finality in minutes" rhythm, not a new problem). "A recovery block never expires" refers precisely to the landing validity of \texttt{R} (tier (ii) has no gap cap, so \texttt{R} can land whenever it is proved), not to \texttt{R} being extendable without maintenance. The exact condition for recovery liveness is therefore not "one builder produces one block" but "lookahead builders keep returning at a density of ≥ 1 per \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} window" --- that is, the \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} liveness floor that normal block production already requires. If only a single builder returns and its lookahead turns are sparser than once per \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max}: nobody extends \texttt{R} within \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} → \texttt{R} itself freezes (\hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}) → the next builder produces another \texttt{R'} on the same old landing head (\texttt{R} is unlanded and not final, so it cannot be referenced by tier (ii)). This retry loop is benign and loses no ground (the landing head has not moved, and each attempt re-roots from the same point); once participation returns above the \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} floor, some \texttt{R} is extended, lands, and recovery completes. This floor is identical to the normal block-production floor and is not a new requirement specific to recovery; a genuine long-term outage of all participants is a matter for social recovery and is out-of-model. Forced-only continuation is analogous: under a full cartel, forced-only blocks from any key must likewise be produced continuously at a density of ≥ 1 per \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} in order to drain the backlog, and a single block is not enough to sustain progress.
\item \textbf{Why a stall of any duration can be recovered in one hop (this is the fundamental improvement over the old design)}: tier (ii) imposes no cap in the gap dimension, so a recovery block does not expire because its parent distance is too large (there is no "expiry" in the gap dimension). Whether the stall lasted 2 hours or 2 days, recovery is always "produce one block with the landing head as parent → one round of proving → landing". The whole family of problems from the old design in which "proving latency blows through the gap freshness window" (r28 / round 7 P1) therefore disappears. Note the distinction of dimensions: what is stated here is that the gap does not expire; the other dimension, anchor freshness, still applies (see the next item + \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}) --- "never expires" refers precisely to the former, not the latter, and the two are not in conflict.
\item \textbf{The precise boundary of "one hop" --- the transient \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} wait \revnote{126}}: the parent of a tier (ii) block must be a final landing head, whereas \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} lets only batches that extend the current canonical landing tip land. The two fail to coincide in exactly one transient: the stall begins shortly after a landing and the current tip has not yet reached \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1}. In that case the recovery block can only wait for the current tip to become final (an extra ≤ \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1}, on the order of minutes) --- building on an older final head would fork off the newer tip and \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} would refuse the landing. The precise recovery bound is therefore max(0, \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} + \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} − the time the current tip has already existed) + one round of proving and landing \revnote{127}: a steady-state disaster stall (in which the last landing is long past that point) degenerates to a pure one hop, while the worst transient, a stall starting immediately after a landing, adds a wait of ≤ \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} + \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} (≈ 19.2 minutes, on the order of minutes). In either case this is independent of the total stall duration (it does not grow with 2 hours or 2 days).
\item \textbf{The constraint anchor freshness places on the recovery block --- "never expires" needs a qualifier \revnote{128}}: what tier (ii) eliminates is gap expiry (no gap cap, so \texttt{R} is valid however far its parent is), but anchor freshness (\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max}) still applies: \texttt{R} pins its anchor at signing time and must land within \texttt{landing L1 block height − anchor ≤ D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max}, failing which \texttt{R}'s anchor is too stale, does not pass \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, and must be re-signed and re-proved against a fresh anchor. The precise statement is therefore: "a recovery block never expires because of the gap" holds, but it must be proved and landed within the \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} freshness window of its anchor. The key difference from the old re-anchor s\_ra: the old \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}ra} freshness window was structurally pinned to \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} (a 64-second wall-clock window), which proving latency (10–15 minutes) necessarily blows through, whereas \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} is a freely settable parameter, and its setter invariant (the full formula is in \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12})\revnote{129} guarantees that a recovery block does not expire under normal L1 liveness --- the old design lost because its window was structurally too small, not because of the mechanism itself. Since r41, the stale-anchor tail deadlock is resolved by the window geometry of \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} (\texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max ≥ D\_\allowbreak{}anchor +\allowbreak{} Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final +\allowbreak{} P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} margin})\revnote{130}. If L1 censors \texttt{R}'s landing transaction continuously for longer than \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} and \texttt{R} has not been committed, \texttt{R} is rebuilt and re-proved with a fresh anchor (with no loss of sequencing) --- that case is a degradation of L1 liveness and does not occur under the \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1} assumption that L1 is safe and live.
\item \textbf{Determinism and benign stranding (designer self-review round 1)}: the parent of a tier (ii) block = a final landing block (having reached the \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} finality depth, so the predicate is stable under shallow L1 reorgs; linked to the L1-reorg item of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}); determinism at landing time is backstopped by the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} landing rule --- a batch may only extend the current canonical landing tip. If a recovery block built on an old tip finds that the tip has already been advanced → benign stranding, and it need only be rebuilt (there is no episode or deposit that can go wrong). And the tip being advanced ⇔ a new landing occurred ⇔ the chain has not truly stopped; during a genuine stall the tip does not move and the recovery block necessarily lands.
\item \textbf{L1-sync and the forced backlog catch up under per-block caps (designer self-review rounds 4/5)\revnote{131}}: "one-hop recovery" refers precisely to L2 sequencing recovering in one hop; the L1→L2 messages and the forced-entry backlog accumulated during a long stall catch up under per-block caps: \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} forced entries per block (\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} for the bridge, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}), and the L1→L2 message-processing cursor \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed} advancing by ≤ \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} messages per block (\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} --- \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} is bound to the message cursor; the anchor reference itself has no per-block advancement cap and may jump to a fresh height in one hop; the earlier text of this line, "the anchor's L1 advancement is ≤ C\_anchor per block", was a leftover in conflict with \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} and has been corrected). The full accounting is therefore: sequencing = 1 hop; L1-sync and the forced backlog = \texttt{backlog /\allowbreak{} per-\allowbreak{}block cap} blocks (after recovery, at 1 block per second, this is usually cleared in seconds to minutes).
\item \textbf{The exact condition for recovery liveness (designer self-review round 2)\revnote{132}}: a tier (ii) content recovery block still requires \texttt{signer = lookahead(\allowbreak{}slot)}, so the exact precondition for discretionary recovery = lookahead builders keep returning at a density of ≥ 1 per \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} window (= the \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} liveness floor of normal block production, not something specific to recovery; a single sparsely returning builder can only re-root and cannot sustain progress, see the "'One hop' means re-rooting" item below); the censorship floor is backstopped by forced-only blocks (from any key), so forced content can be pushed even if all builders are down. An outage of all participants (for example a network-wide client bug) is a matter for social recovery and is out-of-model, as it is for any chain. A targeted DoS against near-future lookahead builders merely postpones recovery by \texttt{≈1/\allowbreak{}p} slots, which is of the same order as the normal residual risk of \hyperref[sec:3.2]{\S3.2} and is not a new hole.
\item \textbf{Deletion list (relative to v1.30)}: the entire \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} episode --- announcement/\texttt{B\_\allowbreak{}ra}, existence challenge/\texttt{B\_\allowbreak{}ch}/deposit settlement, parent pinning/re-pinning, freshness floor/\texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}base}/\texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}ra}, \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}cont} continuation authorization, and the episode state machine --- is deleted in full. The review to-dos that disappear with it: r28 (freshness cannot keep up with landing), round 7 P1 (\texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}cont} shorter than proving), and r29-P2 (three contradictions in deposit settlement / no unique winner with multiple \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}ch} / a second settlement after \texttt{Executed}) --- these mechanisms no longer exist, so the problems no longer apply. \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} is amended accordingly.
\item \textbf{The recovery accounting is given in \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}} (one hop + one round of proving, including a single hop for a long stall; L1-sync catches up according to the backlog). The residual risk is given in \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4} (single-block orphaning via the landing head ≤ the unlanded tail ≤ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}, which takes effect only under a colluding lander and has the same bound as the existing worst case). The lander's tail-selection strategy is given in \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}.
\end{itemize}
\subsection[Aggregator Seat and Economics]{Aggregator Seat and Economics}\label{sec:6.5}
\begin{itemize}
\item The seat is produced by a perpetual auction (inheriting the v15 \hyperref[sec:4]{\S4} skeleton: bonded bidding, the standby queue, the \texttt{q} delayed transition, bonds, and re-auction upon \texttt{T\_\allowbreak{}max} expiry --- all carried over, with the semantics of \texttt{q} and \texttt{T\_\allowbreak{}max} unchanged).
\item \textbf{The fee source must not form a circular dependency with liveness} \revnote{133}: if the service fee comes from the protocol fee stream, then when the chain is idle the fee stream dries up and creates the loop "underpayment → rationally going offline → dependence on the fallback"; the fallback reward is therefore paid solely from the aggregator's bond (already the case, \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}), and the design of the service-fee source (\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 4) must guarantee that the aggregator still breaks even when the fee stream is insufficient (for example, a minimum service fee backstopped by the treasury). The combination of winning the seat cheaply and then slacking off is already closed off by the strike mechanism of \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} (slacking off = strikes = replacement), so all that needs to be handled here is the "honest but underpaid" case.
\item \textbf{The direction of payment is reversed, stated explicitly}: in v15 the seat paid the treasury because it held an exclusive sequencing right; in this design the seat is a service post (batching + proving + L1 gas), and what is auctioned is the lowest service fee rate (a reverse auction, with the service fee paid out of protocol fee revenue), with the bond accounted for separately. The bidding dimensions are the pair (service fee rate, bond): the lower rate wins, and at equal rates the higher bond wins. Refinement is a calibration item of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}.
\item MEV and priority fees go to the builder of each slot (coinbase). The aggregator does not touch sequencing and therefore structurally has no MEV position --- this is intentional.
\item \textbf{Base-fee share --- a positive incentive to land a longer chain \revnote{134}}: a fixed proportion \texttt{φ\_\allowbreak{}land} (a \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} calibration item) of the sum of the base fees of all blocks in the candidate batch serves as the landing reward and is credited, in the atomic commit at the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window close, to the beneficiary address of the closing winner (the beneficiary is part of the proof's public inputs, per the anti-plagiarism clause of \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}, so mempool plagiarism cannot change where it points); the remaining base fee follows the current protocol's destination. Three immediate corollaries: (i) the base of the share grows monotonically with chain length --- landing a longer chain yields a larger share, which closes the loop with \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}'s "landing the longest visible chain is the rational choice"; (ii) a provisional lander superseded by a heavier candidate receives nothing (winner takes all) --- the opportunity cost of landing a short chain = the entire share + the gas and proving fees already paid; (iii) the share is accounted atomically at close together with the winner's final tuple, and requires no extra transaction or after-the-fact claim. The payment point is the close, not the provisional landing \revnote{135}: a provisional candidate can be superseded within the window, so paying on landing would require a clawback from the superseded lander, whereas paying at close is equivalent to "the last step of the landing procedure settles the accounts automatically" and needs no clawback --- the cash flow of users and landers differs only by one \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle}. The existing \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} rule that "the reward is charged by the number of slots that a landing advances" (reward anti-grinding) continues to apply to the service-fee component; the base-fee share is a content-volume incentive layered on top of it, and the two point in the same anti-grinding direction (a micro-batch neither suppresses the fallback window nor has much of a share base).
\end{itemize}
\section[Forced inclusion: retained, with the rules simplified to a per-block obligation]{Forced inclusion: retained, with the rules simplified to a per-block obligation}\label{sec:7}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{The queue semantics are carried over, but the data carrier changes to calldata \revnote{136}}: users submit forced entries (transactions or bridge messages) to the L1 contract via \texttt{saveForcedInclusion} and pay a base fee; the semantics of fees, size caps and per-source isolation are carried over from the current deployment. The data carrier, however, must change: the current queue stores only a blob reference (\texttt{LibBlobs.BlobReference}), and blobs expire after roughly 18 days --- the \texttt{init3} of the deployed contract had to void old entries precisely because blobs expire. The \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} escape path of this design is meant to serve as a fallback exactly during a long stall (possibly longer than the blob retention period), so the payload of a forced entry is instead carried as calldata on the submitting transaction, and the contract stores its content hash at enqueue time: the payload is permanently reconstructible from L1 history (calldata is part of chain history and does not expire), the proof of a forced-only block need only open the payload against the stored hash, and the bound \texttt{≤ F\_\allowbreak{}delay +\allowbreak{} H\_\allowbreak{}force} no longer depends on the blob retention period. Forced entries are usually very small (a single transaction or a single bridge message), so the calldata cost can be covered by the base fee; for entries that genuinely require a large payload, a secondary channel can be added along the lines of "blob carrier + must be included before expiry, otherwise voided and refunded" (whether this is needed is a \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} calibration item). This is the same technique that v15 \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} applies to bridge entries with "L1-authoritative calldata + on-chain hash", and it incidentally removes the main trigger surface of the whole voiding/refund/recall machinery that existed in v15 because of blob expiry.
\item \textbf{Inclusion rule (circuit-enforced, per block, with a capacity cap)}: an entry becomes due \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}delay} after submission. Any block whose slot is \texttt{s} must include, in queue order and as a prefix at the head of the block, those entries that are "due at slot ≤ s and not yet included on this chain" --- up to that block's forced-inclusion capacity cap \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} (a consensus constant capped jointly by gas and by entry count); the remainder that does not fit stays due and is consumed in order by subsequent blocks (including forced-only blocks). \textbf{A block whose head prefix is below the cap yet omits a due entry is invalid} (the precise form of \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 4) --- a block produced by a builder that censors forced entries is simply void, which is equivalent to abstaining and creating a gap.
