Why a cap is mandatory \revnote{137}: v1.3 stated "must include all of them", with no cap --- an attacker need only accumulate, before some slot, more due entries than a single block's capacity in order to make every block (including forced-only blocks, to which \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 4 applies equally) permanently unable to "include all of them" and therefore collectively invalid, so that the chain stalls permanently after \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}delay}. The cap plus deterministic overflow is exactly the per-snapshot capacity cap and overflow rule of v15 \hyperref[sec:6.5]{\S6.5}/\hyperref[sec:6.8]{\S6.8} ("the excess deterministically overflows into the next epoch") transplanted to per-block granularity --- v1.3 dropped this half of the rule when transplanting it. The lower bound on the drain rate of the backlog is \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} per block, so piling up a backlog only buys the attacker delay; it cannot buy a stall. The time bound is backlog-aware, not constant \revnote{138}: the execution time bound of entry \texttt{i} = \texttt{time of becoming due +\allowbreak{} ceil(\allowbreak{}number of due,\allowbreak{} unconsumed entries ahead of it /\allowbreak{} C\_\allowbreak{}force) × landing cadence} --- when the backlog is bounded it degenerates back to a constant bound; an attacker who keeps enqueueing at high speed and paying for it can lengthen the wait of later entries (economic DoS resistance: the cost grows linearly with the number of entries and buys no stall, only in-order delay). Reserved quota for safety messages --- a two-queue implementation \revnote{139}: the forced-inclusion queue is split into two independent FIFOs --- an ordinary queue (cursor \texttt{f\_\allowbreak{}cur\_\allowbreak{}ord}) and a bridge/exit queue (cursor \texttt{f\_\allowbreak{}cur\_\allowbreak{}br}), with membership determined at enqueue time by entry type (bridge messages are recognized via the L1-authoritative entry point). The canonical order of the forced prefix of each block: first take due entries from the bridge queue up to \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} (initially = 25\% × \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}), then take due entries from the ordinary queue to fill up to \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}; each of the two cursors advances under the atomic rule of r7-3, and determinism and circuit-verifiability are unchanged. The time bound of a bridge entry therefore depends only on the backlog of the bridge queue itself, and is unaffected by a flood of ordinary entries; this works together with the existing per-source isolation and base fee. All time bounds in the \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} table that involve the forced path are to be read according to the backlog-aware formula (for the respective queue). Queue cursor semantics \revnote{140}: "not yet included on this chain" is stated precisely via the queue cursor \texttt{f\_\allowbreak{}cursor} (the total number of entries this chain has consumed): the cursor is part of the proven chain state; the forced prefix of each block consumes exactly the due entries at queue positions \texttt{[f\_\allowbreak{}cursor,\allowbreak{} f\_\allowbreak{}cursor +\allowbreak{} k)} (\texttt{k} takes the maximum value that is "due and permitted by capacity", \texttt{k ≤ C\_\allowbreak{}force}) and advances the cursor --- a later block within the same batch reads the cursor as advanced by the earlier blocks, not the L1 snapshot taken at the start of the batch, so there is neither double-counting of an obligation nor a missed determination within a batch. Atomic state transition \revnote{141}: the L1 contract maintains the canonical pair of consumed cursors \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}consumed = (\allowbreak{}f\_\allowbreak{}cur\_\allowbreak{}ord,\allowbreak{} f\_\allowbreak{}cur\_\allowbreak{}br)} (kept separate from the enqueue tail cursors of the two queues). At landing time: (1) the starting cursor pair declared by the batch must equal the contract's current \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}consumed} componentwise; (2) the public input of the proof is the queue snapshot = (the two queue roots, the two tail cursors). The net rule (implement exactly this one sentence)\revnote{142}: \textbf{the forced-inclusion obligation of each block is computed per block and proven per block against [the anchor already committed to in that block's signed header]}; the "batch-level snapshot" here refers specifically to the pair of consumed cursors \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}consumed} (matched componentwise at the start when landing on L1, and advanced atomically to the end value), not a second due-determination scheme independent of the per-block anchor --- dueness is always judged against each block's own anchor, and the cursor pair records only "how far consumption has reached". The snapshot binding is changed to be per block and written into the signed block header \revnote{143}: previously r18-2 fixed the snapshot as "the anchor height \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}batch} of the first block of the batch" (unique per batch), but Codex pointed out in round 18 that this makes the forced-inclusion obligation of an already-signed block depend on the batch boundary the lander ultimately chooses --- a signed P2P tail can be split and landed at any prefix, and if \texttt{B2}'s \texttt{forced\_\allowbreak{}root}/state transition was signed against snapshot \texttt{H1} while the lander lands only \texttt{B1}, making \texttt{B2} the first block of the next batch (whose snapshot becomes \texttt{H2}), then \texttt{B2} either omits the entries that became due in \texttt{H1→H2}, or cannot be proven within a batch that starts at \texttt{B1}; and since \texttt{forced\_\allowbreak{}root} is already signed and immutable, the tail cannot be repaired, so landing an ordinary prefix would strand a block that was otherwise legal. The fix: the forced-inclusion obligation of each block is computed against the anchor it has itself committed to in its signed block header (the \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} block header already contains an anchor reference) --- the obligation is fixed at signing time and is independent of batch boundaries; the batch proof verifies the forced prefix of each block against that block's own anchor. Because the anchor is non-decreasing along the chain, \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}consumed} still advances monotonically (each block consumes the prefix that is "due against this block's anchor and beyond the cursor"). The freshness check at the L1 entry point must be performed per block \revnote{144}, against \texttt{landing L1 block height − block anchor ≤ D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max}, which still seals off "hiding due entries behind a stale anchor", and now the same signed block has the same obligation no matter which batch boundary it lands at, and cannot be stranded. The exit time bound remains the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} backlog formula \texttt{+\allowbreak{} D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} (bounded). (3) Once verification passes, the contract atomically advances the whole \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}consumed} pair to the end cursor pair of the batch --- as amended by r42: both the check and the advance follow the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window state machine: candidate verification is against the cursor pair of the window's frozen baseline (all candidates in the same window share the same starting point), verification records only the candidate's own end cursor pair, and at window close only the winner's end cursor pair is committed atomically; (4) concurrent candidates are no longer resolved by "the first to land wins", but by the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} total order within the same window superseding one another, with the winner determined at window close. The single-cursor phrasing of r5-6 above is to be read componentwise (each queue "consumes its due prefix and advances its own cursor").
\item The forced consumption at the head of every block, in one diagram (the "uniform maximum prefix" rule, consistent with \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 4 and the model in \hyperref[app:C]{Appendix~C}):
\end{itemize}
\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}
  \node[scnode, scfill, text width=108mm] (Q)
        {Head-of-block consumption: two FIFO queues, an obligation on every block};
  \node[scnode, text width=108mm, below=8mm of Q] (BR)
        {Bridge-queue prefix:\\
         the longest prefix that satisfies both a count $\le C_{\mathrm{bridge}}$\\
         and a cumulative declared gas $\le$ the $C_{\mathrm{bridge}}$ gas share (reserved quota)};
  \node[scnode, text width=108mm, below=8mm of BR] (OR)
        {Ordinary forced-inclusion queue prefix:\\
         the longest prefix with a count $\le C_{\mathrm{force}} - $ bridge entries already taken\\
         and cumulative gas $\le$ the $C_{\mathrm{force}}$ gas share $-$ gas already used by the bridge};
  \node[scnode, text width=108mm, below=8mm of OR] (R)
        {Taking the longest prefix is valid; taking less than it is not\\
         (the rule is not ``exactly so many entries''); the remainder\\overflows deterministically into later blocks};
  \node[scnode, scfill, text width=108mm, below=8mm of R] (D)
        {An entry invalid against the pre-state: consume-and-discard\\
         the cursor advances as usual, the entry is not executed};

  \draw[scarr] (Q) -- (BR);
  \draw[scarr] (BR) -- (OR);
  \draw[scarr] (OR) -- (R);
  \draw[scarr] (R) -- (D);
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{How forced entries are consumed at the head of every L2 block: the bridge queue first takes its longest prefix within the reserved quota, the ordinary forced-inclusion queue then takes its longest prefix within the remaining count and gas quota, and entries that are invalid against the pre-state are handled by consume-and-discard so that the head of the queue cannot deadlock.}
\label{fig:9}
\end{figure}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Admission of illegal entries and consume-and-discard \revnote{145}}: the v1.8 rule creates a dilemma for a malformed or state-illegal entry --- including it violates \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 5 (execution legality), and not including it violates rule 4 (completeness of the forced prefix) --- so a single paid submission could make all ordinary blocks and all forced-only blocks permanently illegal and stall the chain. The fix is two lines of defence, with all determinations deterministic:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Enqueue validation (executed by the L1 contract)}: the payload has been changed to calldata carried by the submitting transaction (the first item of this section), so the contract is able to validate directly at \texttt{saveForcedInclusion} time: that it decodes to a well-formed signed transaction, that the signature is recoverable, that \texttt{chainId} is correct, and that the entry's gas cap does not exceed the per-entry share of the queue it belongs to --- computed as \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force − C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} for ordinary entries and as \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} for bridge entries \revnote{146}. Failing validation means the enqueue is rejected; the cap on bridge entries is enforced in step by the bridge contract when it constructs the message (a setter invariant: the maximum gas of a bridge message ≤ the \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} per-entry share). The setter invariant for lowering capacity parameters \revnote{147}: a lowering of \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}/\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} is refused unless the new per-entry share is ≥ the maximum gas among the unconsumed entries of the corresponding queue. The implementation uses a watermark: each queue maintains, at enqueue time, the maximum gas among its unconsumed entries (reset when the queue drains), and the setter checks against the watermark --- conservative but O(1) and directly readable on L1. Consequently, in any reachable state there is no entry that is "already enqueued yet unable to fit within its quota", and no borrowed-allowance special case is needed. Bridge queue entries are constructed by the bridge contract, so well-formedness is guaranteed by construction.
\item \textbf{Discard at consumption time (consume-and-discard, a circuit rule)}: enqueue validation cannot stop state-dependent illegality (nonce mismatch, or a balance insufficient to cover the gas cap × the fee-rate cap --- state changes after enqueue). The rule: when a block's forced prefix reaches an entry, if that entry does not satisfy the deterministic executability test against the state preceding the point of consumption, then the entry is consumed as usual (the cursor advances as usual and it counts towards \texttt{f\_\allowbreak{}consumed}) but is not executed (nothing changes state except the consumption record). The test is a pure function of the preceding state, so it is circuit-verifiable and identical across all nodes; the legality of a block is thereby decoupled from the content of the entries --- rule 4 requires "consumption in order", not "successful execution". This is the only part of the "per-source default disposition" duty of v15's default derivation rule that must be retained: v2 deletes the entire default-derivation mechanism (the \hyperref[sec:0]{\S0} table), but "deterministic discard of illegal input" is not a derivation policy, it is a completeness requirement, and it is recovered here in minimal form. User-side semantics: the forced-inclusion fee is charged at enqueue time and is not refunded on discard --- format and signature have already been validated at enqueue time, so the remaining discard cases can only stem from the submitter having changed its own nonce or balance after enqueueing. \textbf{Nonce preemption weakens the forced path --- a blocking open item \revnote{148}}: a forced entry uses the ordinary L2 account nonce, and a discard is not refunded. If the user subsequently (for example, to keep operating during a period of censorship) sends another transaction with the same nonce and it gets included first, the forced entry will be discarded at its point of consumption because of the nonce conflict --- a censoring party can deliberately accept a conflicting transaction in order to void the victim's forced entry, weakening a path that ought to be unconditional (the \hyperref[sec:5]{\S5} bridge/exit messages are especially sensitive to this). This is not merely user error; it is a real attack surface against the censorship-resistance floor. Candidate fixes (choose one; requires design work): (i) forced entries occupy a separate per-source forced nonce/counter, disjoint from the ordinary nonce space; (ii) enqueueing freezes that nonce, and ordinary transactions may not preempt it until the forced entry has been consumed; (iii) bridge/exit messages are already constructed by the bridge contract and do not use the user's nonce (the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} bridge queue), so extend that protection to a broader forced class. Raised to a blocking open item (\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}, item 12): until one is chosen and formalized, the unconditionality of the forced path against nonce preemption does not hold, and the forced-inclusion promise made to users must be discounted accordingly.
\end{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Forced-only block --- the censorship floor under a total cartel, now a direct application of \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} tier (ii) (the v1.31 direction-B simplification: the two-sided \texttt{lag > Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall} condition, the announce/challenge/\texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}ra} windows, \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}base}/\texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}ra} and \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}cont} continuation are all deleted --- these go away together with the \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} episode)}:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Scenario}: every builder colludes and produces no discretionary block at all (the out-of-model case of \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}). Forced content still needs a path in.
\item \textbf{Definition}: a forced-only block = anchor (a system-level implicit transaction, \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}) + due forced entries (in queue order, up to the per-block caps \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}/\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge}, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}), with no other discretionary content; signed by an arbitrary key (the circuit verifies validity and waives the lookahead check); the parent rule is given by predicate 2 (re-rooting uses a tier (ii) final landed head, and subsequent blocks use tier (i) to chain onto the previous forced-only block)\revnote{149}. \texttt{coinbase} = a beneficiary address of the producer's own choosing (which collects the forced-inclusion fees), carried as a public input of the batch proof, so that mempool copying cannot redirect it (the v15 I8 pre-committed beneficiary).
\item \textbf{The legality predicate \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced} (precise and circuit-verifiable, replacing the old "three conditions" reference of \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1})\revnote{150}}: a block is accepted by the circuit as a forced-only block if and only if all of the following hold:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{The signature is valid} (an arbitrary key, waiving the \texttt{signer = lookahead(\allowbreak{}slot)} lookahead check --- this is its only waiver relative to \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 1; the signature must still cover the block header and be directly verifiable on L1);
\item \textbf{The parent satisfies one of the following \revnote{151}}:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(re-rooting) tier (ii)}: the parent is a final L1-landed block (at \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} depth), and at landing time it must be the current canonical landed tip (\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}) --- used as the starting point of a forced-only chain, or for re-rooting after the previous forced-only chain has frozen (> \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max});
\item \textbf{(same-lane continuation) tier (i)}: the parent is a previous forced-only block with \texttt{slot − parent.slot ≤ G\_\allowbreak{}max} (the parent itself satisfies \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced}) --- this lets forced-only blocks be chained under a total cartel at a density of \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} per block, continuing to the next block without waiting for each block's \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} finalization, which is what makes the per-block drain at \texttt{backlog/\allowbreak{}C\_\allowbreak{}force} hold. The parent is restricted to be a forced-only block (not an arbitrary tier (i) parent) in order to prevent an arbitrary key from injecting itself, via tier (i), into an honest lookahead tail --- forced-only blocks chain only within the forced-only lane, and an honest content tail is still extended only by lookahead builders under \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 1; the two lanes are separated by "whether the parent satisfies \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced}", and since under the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} total order the content lane globally takes priority over the forced-only lane, even a heavier forced-only chain cannot beat a healthy content tail \revnote{152}.
\end{itemize}
\item \textbf{\texttt{slot} is legal}: \texttt{parent.slot < slot} and \texttt{slot ≤ L2\_\allowbreak{}slot(\allowbreak{}timestamp of the L1 block in which it lands)} (per \hyperref[sec:6.1]{\S6.1}, a future slot may not be landed; the L1 entry point and the circuit both prove this) --- this is the mechanically verifiable form of "= the current wall-clock slot", and depends on no registered identity;
\item \textbf{Content is forced-only, and this is opened up only after \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force} has elapsed \revnote{153}}: the block body = anchor (\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}) + the forced prefix in \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} queue order up to the \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}/\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} shares, with no other discretionary transaction; that prefix is non-empty and its queue-head entry is overdue by ≥ \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force} (that is, \texttt{slot ≥ due\_\allowbreak{}slot(\allowbreak{}queue head) +\allowbreak{} H\_\allowbreak{}force}) --- this gives the lookahead builders a window of \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force} in which to include it first, and an arbitrary-key forced-only block is legal only thereafter, consistent with the "overdue fallback" time bounds of \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}/\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}. This is re-verified per block, and lane continuation does not inherit maturity eligibility \revnote{154}: every forced-only block (including a same-lane continuation block) must satisfy \texttt{slot ≥ due\_\allowbreak{}slot(\allowbreak{}that queue head) +\allowbreak{} H\_\allowbreak{}force} for the queue head it itself consumes; lane continuation waives only the parent's final check, not the per-block entry-maturity check;
\item \textbf{Message consumption + causal ordering}: L1→L2 messages are consumed under the uniform maximum prefix of \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} (entry count ≤ \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} and cumulative gas ≤ the share)\revnote{155}; and the \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} causal-ordering invariant \texttt{anchor.L1\_\allowbreak{}timestamp ≤ L2 timestamp(\allowbreak{}slot)} is satisfied. \textbf{How is slot uniqueness guaranteed \revnote{156}}: because a forced-only block is signed with an arbitrary key, it does not rely on equivocation slashing to guarantee "at most one block per slot" (there is no registered identity to slash). Several legal forced-only blocks may appear in the same slot = competing branches, resolved by the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} fork choice + atomic landing picking one (the first to land is canonical, \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}) --- the same resolution path as an ordinary fork, so slot uniqueness is not needed as a circuit invariant. The non-empty prefix of predicate 4 together with the \texttt{slot ≤ wall clock} of predicate 3 ensures that it is legal only when "there is due forced content and it does not run ahead of the future".
\end{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Legality and competition, folded uniformly into the \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4} residual risk (eliminating the old design's two-sided condition and the finding-6 front-running concern)}: a forced-only block behaves like any tier (ii) block --- in normal operation it is a short branch off the final landed head, and an honest lander does not follow it and does not land it under the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} fork choice, so it does not compete for position with normal content blocks in flight (this is exactly the mutual exclusion that the old design laboriously maintained with the two-sided \texttt{lag > Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall} condition, now given naturally by the fork choice, with no extra switch needed); only a colluding lander would land its isolated tail = the explicitly accepted residual risk of \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4} (same bound). Under a total cartel it is the only chain making progress, and finality is advanced by permissionless landing.
\item \textbf{A unilateral Byzantine aggregator can source an isolating branch by itself --- a tightening of the residual risk that must be stated explicitly \revnote{157}}: the arbitrary-key waiver means that a malicious aggregator can itself construct this tier-(ii) forced-only branch (it first enqueues a forced entry of its own and waits for \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}delay} to elapse, at which point it has a legal non-empty prefix), without suborning any lookahead builder and without producing any slashable equivocation. Hence the "requires lander collusion" residual risk of \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4} can have its "collusion" collapse to a single untrusted aggregator acting alone (it is both the branch author and the lander), rather than the two parties "a malicious builder + another colluding lander". \textbf{As of r41, this attack is self-defeating rather than a residual risk}: (a) it cannot move a healthy tail --- under the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} total order the content lane globally takes priority over the forced-only lane (and the lane is fixed at the first block of the fork, so ordinary child blocks cannot whitewash it); (b) the forced-only candidate it races to land is merely one candidate within a \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window --- a healthy content tail landed into the same window by anyone with a proof simply wins on the total order, and the malicious candidate has wasted its gas + proving fee. "A unilateral, unslashable, repeatable attack" becomes "repeating it necessarily loses money". The residual risk narrows to a single condition: \textbf{nobody lands an honest tail within the window} (fallback feasibility, the same condition as \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}). The discretionary transactions of an isolated tail return to the mempool as usual and are not destroyed.
\item \textbf{Censorship time bound}: a forced entry is included within \texttt{backlog /\allowbreak{} C\_\allowbreak{}force} blocks after "somebody produces a block containing it"; the block containing it may be an ordinary block (the forced-prefix obligation, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}) or a forced-only block (under a total cartel). When that containing block is a tier (ii) block off the landed head (the total-cartel recovery path), its production inherits the \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} recovery bound, including a tip-finality wait of ≤ \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} that stacks in the worst case of a stop-start immediately after landing \revnote{158} --- this term is absent in steady state. The forced-only path is permissionless --- under a total cartel anyone holding an arbitrary key can produce a forced-only block and make progress, so the censorship floor does not depend on any registered identity or on any aggregator. This inherits the intuition of the current deployment's \texttt{permissionlessInclusionMultiplier} and of v15 I6 (forced content always flows), replacing v15's entire anarchy mode with the two-tier parent rule + the forced-prefix obligation.
\end{itemize}
\item \textbf{Why the queue is retained} (the conclusion of \hyperref[sec:6]{\S6} of the assessment document still holds under this design, and is cheaper here): rotation gives a bounded wait when there is "≥ 1 honest builder", but gives zero guarantee against a total cartel, and a cartel cannot be detected on-chain; bridge messages need a path in that does not depend on any operator. In this design the day-to-day cost of the queue is close to zero (it is just one rule in \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}), and the disaster tail (the bridge recall handshake of v15 \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}, and the like) shrinks dramatically because there is no "void and refund" path: an entry is either included, or included by anyone after waiting \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force} --- with no voiding branch, no termination/cancellation handshake is needed either. (The blob expiry problem does not exist: the data of a forced entry is on L1 to begin with.)
\end{itemize}
\section[Anchoring and the bridge (L1 → L2)]{Anchoring and the bridge (L1 → L2)}\label{sec:8}
\begin{itemize}
\item The current per-block anchoring pattern is carried over: the first transaction of every block is the anchor transaction (anchor tx), which references an L1 block at depth ≥ \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} (32 L1 slot), is non-decreasing relative to the previous anchor, and whose freshness does not exceed the cap (the numerical values carry over the shape of the v15 \hyperref[sec:6.6]{\S6.6} constraints).
\item \textbf{The anchor may not be later than this block's slot time --- the L1→L2 causal-ordering invariant \revnote{159}}: in addition to being non-decreasing and fresh, it must also hold that \texttt{the anchor's L1 timestamp ≤ the L2 timestamp corresponding to this block's slot} (equivalently, \texttt{L2\_\allowbreak{}slot(\allowbreak{}anchor.L1\_\allowbreak{}timestamp) ≤ slot}), and this applies to ordinary blocks and forced-only blocks alike. Why it is necessary: \hyperref[sec:6.1]{\S6.1} rule 3 only blocks "future slots", and \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} only blocks "an anchor that is too old", and neither blocks "an old slot paired with a new anchor" --- a builder could hold back an old slot, then pick after the fact a fresh anchor that has just arrived and that already contains some new L1→L2 message, and sign a tier (i) continuation that consumes that message into an L2 block whose slot timestamp is earlier than the L1 block the message originated in; a Byzantine aggregator lands it ahead of honest recovery → L1→L2 causal ordering is broken, and the bridge/message deadline semantics based on \texttt{block.timestamp} are distorted. With this invariant added, a block can never reference L1 state from after its own slot time. It does not affect recovery: a recovery block references an L1 block at depth ≥ \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} (≈ 6.4 minutes ago), whose timestamp is always ≤ the current wall-clock slot time, so an honest block satisfies it naturally; this invariant rejects only the malformed combination "old slot + new anchor". The L1 entry point and the circuit both prove it (\texttt{anchor.L1\_\allowbreak{}timestamp} is a pure function of the referenced L1 block header).
\item The time geometry of the anchor's age along the worst-case path (the origin of the \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} setter invariant, \hyperref[app:C]{Appendix~C} P9a):
\end{itemize}
\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[font=\scriptsize]
  \draw[scarr] (0,0) -- (12.8,0);
  \foreach \x in {1.2,5.0,8.5,12.0}{\draw[scline] (\x,-0.15) -- (\x,0.15);}

  % below the axis: four instants
  \node[scsm, text width=32mm, anchor=north] at (1.2,-0.35)
        {Block produced\\anchor age $\le D_{\mathrm{anchor}}$\\($32$ L1 slot)};
  \node[scsm, text width=26mm, anchor=north] at (5.0,-0.35)
        {Fallback authorized};
  \node[scsm, text width=24mm, anchor=north] at (8.5,-0.35)
        {Proof completed};
  \node[scsm, scfill, text width=32mm, anchor=north] at (12.0,-0.35)
        {Candidate lands\\anchor freshness\\checked here};

  % above the axis: three spans
  \draw[scdim] (1.2,0.5) -- (5.0,0.5);
  \node[sclab, text width=40mm, anchor=south] at (3.1,0.6)
        {aggregator withholds landing\\$\le \Delta_{\mathrm{lag,final}}$\\(fallback-window threshold)};
  \draw[scdim] (5.0,0.5) -- (8.5,0.5);
  \node[sclab, text width=28mm, anchor=south] at (6.75,0.6)
        {proving\\$\le P_{\mathrm{prove,max}}$};
  \draw[scdim] (8.5,0.5) -- (12.0,0.5);
  \node[sclab, text width=32mm, anchor=south] at (10.25,0.6)
        {L1 inclusion\\$\le T_{\mathrm{include,max}}$};

  % whole span + invariant
  \draw[scdim] (1.2,-2.55) -- (12.0,-2.55);
  \node[scnote, text width=126mm, anchor=north] at (6.4,-2.7)
        {Invariant: $D_{\mathrm{anchor,max}} \ge D_{\mathrm{anchor}} + \Delta_{\mathrm{lag,final}}
         + P_{\mathrm{prove,max}} + T_{\mathrm{include,max}} +$ margin\\
         $\approx 420$ L1 slot $\approx 84$ minutes, covering the whole fallback-authorization path, so no deadlock exists};
\end{tikzpicture}
\caption{The time geometry of anchor age along the worst-case path: the freshness window $D_{\mathrm{anchor,max}}$ must cover the whole route from block production, through the aggregator's withholding until the threshold authorizes fallback, and on to proving and L1 inclusion. This relation between parameters is exactly the solution to the stale-anchor tail deadlock.}
\label{fig:10}
\end{figure}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{The solution to the stale-anchor tail deadlock --- \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} covers the fallback authorization period \revnote{160}}: when a malicious aggregator withholds landing for a long time, the anchor of an early block of an honest tail will, "at landing time", exceed \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} → the tail can then neither be landed (its first block is past the deadline) nor be skipped (\texttt{parent\_\allowbreak{}hash} runs through it). The solution is parameter geometry, not a new mechanism: the setter invariant \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max ≥ D\_\allowbreak{}anchor +\allowbreak{} Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final +\allowbreak{} P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} margin} \revnote{161} --- that is, the freshness window must cover the entire course "from block production, through \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final} crossing the threshold and opening the fallback, to the fallback proving and landing it". Consequently, any tail that the fallback is authorized to land (produced while \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final ≤ Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final} and landed by the fallback after the threshold is crossed) necessarily still has a fresh anchor, and no deadlock exists. A rough calculation with the initial parameters: 32 + 288 + 75 + 10 + margin ≈ 420 L1 slot (≈ 84 minutes) \revnote{162}, which is still a deployable value. For situations beyond that window (the fallback too is persistently absent for > D\_anchor\_max), the stale prefix can no longer be landed and the chain is rebuilt from the window-final head --- the same conditional residual risk as \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} fallback feasibility, stated honestly. The causal-ordering invariant (the previous item) is unaffected.
\item Because there is a block every second (under normal conditions), the intake cadence of L1→L2 messages is on the order of seconds --- better than the fallback path of the forced-only cadence in v15. In the gap and cartel cases, bridge messages take the forced path of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}.
\item \textbf{The L1→L2 message-processing cap \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} --- bound to the [processing cursor], not to the anchor reference \revnote{163}}: distinguish two independent quantities:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{The anchor reference} (which L1 block the block references as its freshness baseline): still non-decreasing + required to be fresh (\texttt{landing L1 block height − anchor ≤ D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max}). A recovery block references a current, fresh anchor --- even if the anchor of its parent (a landed head from days ago) is very old, this one large monotone jump is permitted (it merely references a recent L1 block and satisfies freshness), and the anchor itself carries no per-block advance cap.
\item \textbf{The L1→L2 message-processing cursor \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed}} (a global message sequence number, not an L1 height)\revnote{164}: the unique canonical algorithm = [the uniform maximum prefix] \revnote{165}: L1→L2 messages form a public FIFO queue (the contract maintains a queue root + tail cursor, kept separate from \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed}); every block must consume the longest prefix of that FIFO that satisfies both "entry count ≤ \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor}" and "cumulative declared gas ≤ the L1→L2 message gas share" (restricted to those that have arrived within the height referenced by this block's anchor and are unconsumed; taking that longest prefix is legal, and only taking less than it is illegal --- as in \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 5, with no second "exactly some entry count" criterion). A recovery block references a fresh anchor (spanning days of L1), each block digests this prefix, and the remainder is continued by subsequent blocks (\texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed} advances monotonically). The backlog catch-up bound is correspondingly two-constraint: \texttt{backlog drain time = max(\allowbreak{}ceil(\allowbreak{}entry count /\allowbreak{} C\_\allowbreak{}anchor),\allowbreak{} ceil(\allowbreak{}cumulative gas /\allowbreak{} the G\_\allowbreak{}l1msg share))} blocks (the bounds of \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}/\hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} are to be read this way)\revnote{166}. Without this lower bound, \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed} is monotone yet may stagnate: a builder that keeps processing 0 messages while still using a fresh anchor could censor L1→L2 messages indefinitely, invalidating the \texttt{backlog /\allowbreak{} C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} catch-up bound of \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}/\hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} \revnote{167}. With the lower bound added, the catch-up bound is circuit-enforced and redeemable. Consumption is not execution --- an inbound message that is illegal against the preceding state is consumed-and-discarded under the same consume-and-discard rule as \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}. \textbf{The real anti-censorship bound is \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max}, not \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} alone \revnote{168}}: the maximum prefix only forces "consumption of those that have arrived within the height referenced by this block's anchor", so a builder that pins its anchor at the oldest height freshness permits (\texttt{landed head − D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max}) can legally consume no newer message at all. But this is not indefinite censorship: the anchor must satisfy the freshness lower bound \texttt{anchor ≥ landing L1 block height − D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} and be non-decreasing, so as the landed head advances, that lower bound rises, and a message that arrived at height \texttt{h} is forced into "within the anchor-referenced height" once \texttt{landed head − D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max > h} → it must then be consumed. Hence the worst-case inclusion delay of an L1→L2 message ≈ \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} (freshness forces the anchor frontier forward) + \texttt{backlog /\allowbreak{} C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} (the processing rate once it has entered the to-be-consumed set); the two parameters jointly bound it, not \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} alone. \revnote{169} Why it is split this way \revnote{170}: the \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} tier (ii) recovery block has to reconnect to the wall clock in one jump from a landed head days old. If \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} were imposed on the anchor reference (the error in the original v1.31 text), then the anchor of a recovery block could advance only \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} from its stale parent anchor, would still be stale, and would fail the freshness check --- failing precisely on the long stall it is meant to support \revnote{171}. Once it is rebound to the message-processing cursor: the anchor reference is fresh (satisfying \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}) and L2 ordering recovers in one jump; while a huge volume of L1→L2 messages is processed block by block at \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} per block, so a full sync takes \texttt{message backlog /\allowbreak{} C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} blocks (after recovery, 1 block/second, which is fast). This is the same technique and the same shape as the per-block \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} amortization of the backlog for \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} forced entries (a fresh reference + a rate-limited cursor). \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} is a \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} calibration item.
\end{itemize}
\item \textbf{A shared block-level gas budget + a per-message gas cap --- sealing off the combined-gas chain stall \revnote{172}}: the forced prefix of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} (\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}/\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge}) and the L1→L2 message consumption of this section (\texttt{min(\allowbreak{}C\_\allowbreak{}anchor,\allowbreak{} ...)}) are two mandatory obligations each with its own quantity, but until now there was no shared block gas budget and no per-message gas cap. Counterexample: block gas 30M, the due forced prefix takes exactly 20M, and the head of \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed} is an inbound message that is legal against the preceding state and requires 11M to execute --- rule 4 requires the block to take the full forced prefix and rule 5 requires it to consume that message, for a total of 31M + the fixed anchor overhead > the block cap, while consuming less on either side is also illegal → for ordinary, recovery and forced-only blocks alike there exists no legal state transition, so the chain stalls permanently even with everyone honest, and the bridge/exit cursors die along with it. The fix (fully deterministic, verifiable by both the circuit and L1):
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{A per-item admission gas cap}: inbound L1→L2 messages and forced entries each get a per-item gas cap at enqueue time, and an over-cap item is refused admission (newly added on the message side)\revnote{173};
\item \textbf{A shared block-level budget invariant}: \texttt{the fixed anchor overhead G\_\allowbreak{}anchor +\allowbreak{} the C\_\allowbreak{}force (\allowbreak{}including C\_\allowbreak{}bridge) gas share +\allowbreak{} the L1→L2 message gas share ≤ block\_\allowbreak{}gas\_\allowbreak{}limit}, as a setter invariant on the consensus constants; each of the three parties' "guaranteed capacity after deducting the others' reserved quotas" fits into the current block under any volume of traffic from the others (carrying over the same technique as the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} two-queue reserved quota, extended to three parties);
\item \textbf{Deterministic priority and overflow}: the enforced execution order within a block = anchor → forced prefix (bridge first, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}) → L1→L2 message prefix; each party, following its own cursor, "consumes up to its guaranteed capacity in this block, and the remainder deterministically overflows into subsequent blocks" --- every cursor is advanced only by legal blocks, so a backlog buys delay and cannot buy a chain stall;
\item \textbf{A watermark for lowering parameters}: when \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}/\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor}/any gas share is lowered, the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} watermark invariant carries over (the new share ≥ the maximum gas among the unconsumed entries of the corresponding queue), guaranteeing that in any reachable state there is no item that is "already enqueued yet unable to fit within the guaranteed capacity". The three separately rate-limited obligations are thereby coordinated by one shared budget, and can no longer be combined into a "no legal block" deadlock. The numerical gas shares are folded into \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{itemize}
\section[What happens when each role goes offline (liveness accounting, in a single table)]{What happens when each role goes offline (liveness accounting, in a single table)}\label{sec:9}
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{0.20\linewidth}p{0.11\linewidth}p{0.11\linewidth}X}
\toprule
\textbf{Who goes offline} & \textbf{What the user sees} & \textbf{Recovery mechanism} & \textbf{Recovery time} \\
\midrule
A single builder & Its slot becomes a gap; every other slot proceeds as usual & No recovery is needed; the next slot continues naturally & \textasciitilde{}1 second \\
Consecutive absences ≤ \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max − 1} (63 slots, so the next block's parent distance is ≤ \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max}) & No new blocks for a few seconds & The next builder resumes under \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} tier (i); an absentee that supplies its signature after the fact can also bridge the interval & \textasciitilde{}number of offline builders × 1 second \\
Consecutive absences ≥ \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} slots, extending to hours or days (catastrophic stall) & No new blocks for the duration of the stall & \textbf{Land the best chain first, then recover from its final tip (\hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}/\hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4})\revnote{174}}: if a frozen long tail existed before the stall, the lander lands it first (thereby honoring its preconfirmations), and once it has reached \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} the recovery block takes its tip as parent and is produced under \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} tier (ii); where there is demonstrably no better chain, the recovery block is itself the best chain and lands directly. Landing a worse candidate is superseded inside the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window by a heavier one (self-defeating)\revnote{175} & \textbf{Ordering ≈ one round of proving + landing (≈10–15 minutes, independent of the length of the stall) + the \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} window close \revnote{176} + in the worst case an additional wait of ≤ \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} + \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} when the stall begins immediately after a landing \revnote{177}}; where a frozen long tail exists, this includes one round of proving to "land the tail first"; L1-sync and forced-inclusion backlogs catch up at \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor}/\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} per block; \textbf{sustained progress requires scheduled builders to keep returning at a window density of ≥1/\texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} (the normal-mode \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} liveness floor)\revnote{178}} or the forced-only floor (at the same density) \\
Aggregator goes offline (honest but offline) & \textbf{Nothing perceptible} (preconfirmations proceed as usual); finality is deferred & \texttt{lag > Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} → the fallback window opens, anyone may land and a strike is recorded; \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg} strikes → the standby is promoted & Finality lag is pinned at \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} + the fallback response; 0 interruption of the sequencing service \\
Aggregator online but maliciously stalling (Byzantine, landing micro-batches) & As above & Micro-batches cannot hold lag down → the fallback window opens, strikes are recorded and the seat changes hands exactly as before (\hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}) & As above (\textbf{bounded --- but conditional on permissionless fallback being economically viable})\revnote{179}; the seat changes hands within \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg} strikes \\
Aggregator offline + no standby & As above & Fallback landing remains available throughout (no seat is required); the normal cadence resumes once the auction has cleared & 0 interruption of the sequencing service \\
All builders at once (catastrophe or cartel) --- \textbf{out-of-model} (\hyperref[sec:1]{\S1} trust model) & No new preconfirmations & Once a forced entry is \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force} past due, \textbf{anyone may produce a forced-only block with any key} (\hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} tier (ii) built on the landed head + \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}, defense in depth, no episode); new builders may join the registry at any time & Forced path ≈ the per-queue backlog-aware bound (\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}); discretionary service waits for new builders to join \\
Builders and aggregator colluding as a whole (block bodies kept private) --- \textbf{out-of-model} (\hyperref[sec:1]{\S1} trust model; the owner's decision is not to solve this inside the protocol) & Preconfirmations are still issued but cannot be trusted; finality stalls, and \textbf{correctly} no aggregator strike is produced (the stall is attributed to the cartel) & The forced path is the defense-in-depth exit; any single builder defecting from the collusion immediately produces a landable fork (\hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}, self-healing), which returns the system to the model & Forced path ≈ the per-queue backlog-aware bound (\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}); everything else waits for the collusion to break down \\
Proving-system failure & Preconfirmations proceed as usual; no new finality & \textbf{Zero false positives for fallback strikes holds} (nobody can land ⇒ no strike is recorded, \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}); but the exemption for late fees and late counts \textbf{does not yet exist} --- until \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 8 (blocking) is complete, the first batch landed after recovery is charged under the current rules \revnote{180} & Equal to the duration of the failure; the risk of wrongly penalizing the recovery batch remains open until \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 8 is closed \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

By comparison with v15: there, "one seat goes offline" = a 20–26 minute service interruption (when a standby exists); here the same class of event costs 1 second (a builder) or 0 seconds (an aggregator, which affects only the cadence of finality). This is the reason this design exists.

\section[Master table of slashing and bonds]{Master table of slashing and bonds}\label{sec:10}
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{0.20\linewidth}p{0.22\linewidth}X}
\toprule
\textbf{Fault} & \textbf{How it is adjudicated} & \textbf{Consequence} \\
\midrule
Builder equivocation (two block headers for the same slot) & A challenger submits the two signed block headers and the L1 contract verifies the signatures directly & Slash \texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}eq} (≥80\% burned, the remainder paid to the submitter) + removal from the registry \\
Builder skips the previous block / is absent & Not a fault (producing a block is an opportunity, not an obligation) & No slashing; a skip is \textbf{profitable} for the skipper (fee/MEV transfer)\revnote{181}. Orphaning depth follows the three tiers of \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}: an adjacent skip affects a single slot; under \textbf{tier (i)} a single-block deep skip orphans at most \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max − 1} unlanded blocks, and a minority coalition relaying a sparse branch can reach the entire unlanded tail ≈ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} \revnote{182}; under \textbf{tier (ii)} a single malicious builder can, with one block built on a final landed head, orphan at most the entire unlanded tail ≈ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} \revnote{183}. The worst-case depth is the same in all three tiers, namely \textbf{≤ the actual length of the unlanded tail (≈ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} when the fallback lands on schedule, conditional)\revnote{184}}; in every tier the attacker must beat any heavier honest candidate inside the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window before it can become final, the signature is on record for public attribution, and the frequency is constrained by the honest-majority assumption; an absentee forfeits the fee income of its own slot \\
Builder signs a header but withholds the body (withhold-body, \hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}) & Not adjudicable ("it has the body and is not releasing it" cannot be proven) & No slashing; the block is certain to be skipped and the withholder gains nothing at all; the receipt is itself public evidence, and the response is reputational \\
Aggregator dereliction (a successful landing by someone else while the fallback window is open = a strike, \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}) & Mechanically adjudicated on L1 (the lag state plus the fact that a fallback batch was accepted) & Each strike is penalized at the liveness tier; \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg} accumulated strikes → termination + promotion of the standby \\
Aggregator takes a prefix / lands a worse chain (truncating the suffix) & \textbf{Not a slashable fault \revnote{185}} & No slashing; landing a worse chain is \textbf{self-defeating}: inside the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} settlement window anyone can land a heavier proven candidate and supersede it, so the malicious party has wasted its gas and proving fees; and if only a prefix is taken, the suffix can still enter a subsequent window \\
Anyone submits an invalid batch & The proof fails verification and the transaction reverts & Wasted gas, no protocol consequence \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

The structural property of this table, in the honest formulation adopted after review r1-11: every slashable fault is adjudicated either by direct signature verification on L1 (equivocation) or by a mechanical judgment over the L1 clock and L1 facts (landing strikes) --- there is no class of slashing that requires an L2 evidence chain plus an arbitration period (the watchtower-dependent *slashing* of the v15 \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}b kind does not exist here, because "preconfirmation" and "block" are one and the same signed object, so equivocation is simply signing two headers for one slot and is directly verifiable). There do exist two classes of misbehavior that are not slashable and are only deterred (skipping and body withholding, \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}/\hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}) --- this design does not eliminate them; the precise statement \revnote{186}: withholding a body yields the actor no benefit, whereas skipping is profitable (the fee/MEV transfer of a single slot), and its frequency is constrained by the honest-majority assumption of \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1} --- both leave public signed evidence for attribution.

\section[Detailed comparison with v15 (for readers who have read v15)]{Detailed comparison with v15 (for readers who have read v15)}\label{sec:11}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{What became of v15's nine invariants}: I1 (derivation is a pure function) is inherited and strengthened (timestamps and the lookahead are all pure functions); the spirit of I2 (a single adjudication / computed-state certificate) is inherited by the landing-timeout adjudication, but there is no longer an epoch adjudication object; I3 (the epoch always advances) is replaced by "the L1 chain head can always be advanced by anyone (fallback landing + forced-only blocks)"; I6 (forced content always flows) is inherited (\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}, and enforced more strongly: a per-block circuit obligation); I7 (sealing is sender-agnostic) is generalized into "landing carries no authority"; I8 (pre-committed beneficiary) is inherited (the recipients of the fallback reward and of the slashing bounty are made public inputs of the proof); I9 (safety slashing is denominated in ETH) is inherited by \texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}eq}.
\item \textbf{v15's review legacy is not voided}: the auction skeleton (\hyperref[sec:4]{\S4}), the at-fault-pays model (\hyperref[sec:6.7]{\S6.7}), the anchor freshness constraint (\hyperref[sec:6.6]{\S6.6}), the burn-dominant split of slashed funds (\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}) and the discipline of invariant checks in parameter setters are all carried over directly. The parts of v15 that revolved around "one irreversible adjudication per epoch" (EBC, AC, default derivation, anarchy mode, cancellation cascade) have no counterpart in this design, so the corresponding historical review findings do not apply.
\item \textbf{Response to the four criticisms in the assessment document}: the single point of failure is merely relocated → resolved (landing carries no authority, plus lag-based fallback and strikes, which are bounded for both the offline and the Byzantine aggregator --- \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}); builder faults are all of the watchtower-dependent class → the slashable class is resolved (equivocation is verified directly on L1), but two classes of non-slashable misbehavior (skipping, body withholding) remain as deterrence-grade residual risk and are stated explicitly (\hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}/\hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}) --- this is "downgraded and explicitly priced", not "eliminated"; fair exchange has become load-bearing → partially resolved (intermediate deletion is eradicated by the signature chain; the residual risk is as above); the epoch mechanism needs a rewrite → conceded, and this document is that rewrite, whose result is shorter than v15.
\end{itemize}
\section[Open items]{Open items}\label{sec:12}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Builder-set size and admission rules}: \texttt{N\_\allowbreak{}max}, the weight cap \texttt{w\_\allowbreak{}max} on the bond-weighted sampling, the rules for excess competition, and liveness requirements (whether prolonged absence should reduce weight).
\item \textbf{Pricing of \texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}eq}}: a method for estimating the worst-case extractable value of a single slot, and whether governance should adjust it as market conditions change.
\item \textbf{Landing parameters}: \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov}/\texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final} \revnote{187}, \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg}, \texttt{δ\_\allowbreak{}slash}, \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force}, and the exponential cost cap on the fallback reward; \textbf{recovery parameters (after the v1.31 direction-B redesign)}: \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} (the L1 finality depth of the final landed block in tier (ii)), \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} (the per-block cap on the number of entries consumed by the \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} message-processing cursor \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed} --- not a cap on anchor advancement, since anchor references have no per-block cap)\revnote{188}, \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}prop} (the propagation settling amount of the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} landing depth, normally << \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}) and \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} (the anchor freshness cap)\revnote{189}. \textbf{\texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} setter invariant \revnote{190}: \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max ≥ D\_\allowbreak{}anchor +\allowbreak{} Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final(\allowbreak{}converted to L1 slots) +\allowbreak{} P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} queue/\allowbreak{}clock margin}} \revnote{191} (r42 adds the \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} term and requires every addend to be converted to L1 slots first --- independent review round 5, high-severity finding 3: this line previously omitted \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}, so a legal parameter set such as 130 would make an honest tail that is withheld from landing until the fallback window opens expire with certainty; the contract setter implements only this single strongest invariant. \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor}=32 L1 slot; \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max}=the worst-case proving latency; \texttt{T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max}=the bounded-inclusion bound of \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}. The other setter relation \revnote{192}: \texttt{T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max < min(\allowbreak{}W\_\allowbreak{}settle − P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max − margin,\allowbreak{} D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max − D\_\allowbreak{}anchor − Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag − P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max)}) --- v1.38 once omitted the term expressing that an anchor is already at least \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} old at signing time; writing merely "≥ proving + landing" yields a parameter set that fails permanently even with no attacker present (the reviewer's counterexample: 70 configured, 102 actually required). Otherwise the recovery block (and any block) would have its anchor expire inside the proving-plus-landing window and would have to be re-signed and re-proven (\hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}, anchor freshness clause); this is the precondition for recovery "not expiring because of a gap", and it replaces the 64-second structural window of the old \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}ra} with a free parameter that can be set large enough. Stated honestly: a finite \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} is still a "proving + inclusion delay vs. freshness window" tradeoff, only with a window that can be configured large enough, which is why C1 is explicitly conditional on the bounded-inclusion assumption "temporary L1 congestion or censorship ≤ \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max}" and claims nothing unconditional against censorship of arbitrary length. \textbf{Settlement-window parameters \revnote{193}}: \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} (the length of the settlement window, counted in L1 blocks; setter invariants: the lower bound \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle ≥ P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} margin}, and the consensus upper bound \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle\_\allowbreak{}max} (which includes a margin for wall-clock conversion under missed L1 slots --- the upper bound pins down the revocable exposure \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag +\allowbreak{} W\_\allowbreak{}settle(\allowbreak{}wall clock) +\allowbreak{} fallback response} of \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2})\revnote{194}, with an initial suggestion of ≈ 100 L1 slot ≈ 20 minutes); the L1-reorg rollback of window state is folded into item 9. The semantics of \texttt{final\_\allowbreak{}ref} (a \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} tier-(ii) block-header field, attesting that the parent was already \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1}-final at signing time, already part of the block-header tuple and of \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}). \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}prop} is demoted to an off-chain strategy reference for the completeness of a lander's view (\hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}, not an on-chain attribution parameter). (The \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}chal}/\texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}land}/\texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}chal}/\texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}commit}/\texttt{δ\_\allowbreak{}land} of v1.39/v1.40 were removed together with the challenge and commitment layers and are no longer parameters.) Shared gas shares (\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}): \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}anchor}, the \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}/\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} gas shares and the L1→L2 message gas share, subject to \texttt{sum of the three ≤ block\_\allowbreak{}gas\_\allowbreak{}limit} + a per-entry admission cap + a watermark \revnote{195}. (The \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}ra}/\texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}ra\_\allowbreak{}ext}/\texttt{B\_\allowbreak{}ra}/\texttt{B\_\allowbreak{}ch}/\texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}cont} of the old re-anchor episode were removed together with the episode and are no longer parameters.) The behavior of the strike mechanism under extreme congestion (an L1 fee spike that leaves nobody willing to perform the fallback). \textbf{The concrete bounds given around these parameters in \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} and \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} (\texttt{backlog/\allowbreak{}C\_\allowbreak{}anchor}, recovery time, the \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} anti-censorship bound and so on) are all [provisional values] until the parameters have been calibrated \revnote{196}} --- the form is settled, the numbers are not.
\item \textbf{Details of the aggregator reverse auction}: the funding source for the service fee rate (protocol fees or the treasury), the bid-evaluation function over rate and bond, and the bindingness of the standby queue.
\item \textbf{P2P layer specification}: the propagation protocol for signed blocks, the engineering guarantees for block-body availability (the format of preconfirmation receipts, the relaying obligations among builders, whether erasure-coded propagation is worth it) and public metrics for skipping and body withholding (published as a public dashboard, the lever that makes deterrence bite --- the deterrence-grade residual risk of \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}/\hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5} rests entirely on it).
\item \textbf{Proving-circuit cost}: an assessment of the in-circuit cost of per-block signature verification (at most 384 signatures per epoch) plus recomputation of the lookahead; whether BLS aggregate signatures are worth introducing (replacing per-block ECDSA with an aggregatable signature, lowering both the circuit cost and the potential cost of direct verification on L1 --- this bears on the choice of builder key scheme); and incremental continuation and aggregation for batch proofs \revnote{197}: once the chain head has been advanced by a small batch, a batch already being proven against the old head must be able to reuse the proven prefix and continue from it, rather than being re-proven from scratch --- this is the precondition for defanging the drip-feed stalling tactic.
\item \textbf{Concurrent multi-batch landing and reorgs}: how to handle the gas competition when several parties race to land inside the fallback window (expected to follow the v15 \hyperref[sec:11.3]{\S11.3} conclusion that "the competitors bear their own costs and the chain advances as usual", to be re-checked).
\item \textbf{Exemption mechanism for proving-system outages (blocking)\revnote{198}}: the form follows v15 \hyperref[sec:10.4]{\S10.4} level 3 (independent corroboration + a bounded exemption + an \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}toll\_\allowbreak{}max}-style cap), substantially simplified in the absence of the cancellation cascade, but it requires a dedicated derivation of its own; until that is done, the "zero false positives" claim of \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} is limited to fallback strikes.
\item \textbf{Handling of L1 reorgs}: the rollback semantics for a landed batch that meets an L1 reorg (expected to be considerably simpler than v15 \hyperref[sec:3.1]{\S3.1}/\hyperref[sec:13]{\S13}-S.1, because there is no cross-transaction certificate state machine, but it still has to be written down).
\item \textbf{Migration path}: the upgrade ordering from the current deployment, and from v15, to this design.
\item \textbf{Interaction sequence diagram} \revnote{199}: the interaction of batch landing, equal-height fork resolution, the fallback window and the forced-only-block escape hatch is currently spread across \hyperref[sec:5]{\S5}–\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}, and deserves a single normative sequence diagram.
\item \textbf{Protection of forced entries against nonce preemption (blocking)\revnote{200}}: see \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}; until one of the three options --- a per-source forced nonce, nonce freezing, or a bridge-contract construction --- has been selected and formalized, the forced path is not unconditional against nonce preemption.
\item \textbf{Availability and cost of fallback provers} \revnote{201}: to be folded into the threat model --- the Byzantine-aggregator liveness bound of \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} is conditional on it; and the design of fallback incentives under extreme congestion or when the proving cost exceeds the reward.
\item \textbf{Upper bound on the L1 cost of the \texttt{saveForcedInclusion} enqueue validation} \revnote{202}: decoding a signed L2 transaction on L1, verifying its signature and checking \texttt{chainId} and the share all carry non-trivial gas costs, and they expose a griefing surface against malformed calldata. A gas cap on the validation itself and a cheap rejection path for unparsable submissions (paid for by the submitter) must be defined, or the validation must be pushed down to a cheaper domain.
\item \textbf{Worst-case calibration of the margin on the lag thresholds} \revnote{203}: \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov} (4 epoch ≈25.6 min) leaves only ≈5.6 min of margin over the upper bound of the normal provisional lag band (20 min); for \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final} (≈9 epoch ≈57.6 min)\revnote{204}, the margin over the steady-state \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final} band (≈35–40 min) is carried by the margin term of \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle\_\allowbreak{}max}. A proving spike compounded by jitter in L1 finality could push a conscientious aggregator past the threshold and open the fallback window in error. Whether to widen it needs to be assessed.
\item \textbf{Migration of the forced-inclusion data carrier} \revnote{205}: \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} changes the payload of a forced entry from a blob reference to calldata (permanently reconstructible), but the current \texttt{MainnetInbox} stores a \texttt{LibBlobs.BlobReference}. A migration or compatibility path is needed: how already-enqueued blob-ref entries are handled at the switchover (replayed as calldata, both carriers accepted during a grace period, or the switch deferred until the old queue has drained) --- otherwise entries sitting in the queue at the instant of the upgrade could be stranded.
\item \textbf{Genesis and bootstrap semantics} \revnote{206}: the initial \texttt{lag} (with no landed head, \texttt{lag} may be arbitrarily large and the fallback window is "open" from genesis onwards), the selection of the first aggregator, and strike accounting before the first batch has landed are all undefined. A genesis anchor (a genesis landed head) plus rules suppressing fallback and strikes during the bootstrap period must be defined.
\item \textbf{Strengthening of the formal specification --- the pre-implementation gate \revnote{207}}: \textbf{(delivered, r43)} an executable reference model of the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} total order and the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window state machine, plus property tests (P1–P7, covering every invariant of the two blocking findings of round 5) --- see \hyperref[app:C]{Appendix~C} and \texttt{settlement-\allowbreak{}window-\allowbreak{}model.py}/\texttt{settlement-\allowbreak{}window-\allowbreak{} RESULTS.md}; any change to the rules must be mirrored in the model and the model re-run. (delivered, r44) The second half: the model gains P8–P11 (bridge reservation, anchor geometry and causal ordering, the moment a slashing takes effect, the fallback accounting snapshot; all 19 assertions pass), plus the pre-implementation review document \texttt{\texttt{settlement-\allowbreak{}window-\allowbreak{}implementation-\allowbreak{}review.md}} (Solidity-level \texttt{acceptCandidate} storage and gas, the Inbox integration path, an item-by-item disposition of (a)/(b)/(c) below and a list of what remains open --- of which (b), the precise definition of the lookahead sampling operator, is still open and must be closed before electing is implemented; the final judgment is the owner plus a human safety review, and this gate does not pass anything automatically). The original list: (a) a single set of pseudocode or state machines for block legality / parent selection / the best-chain total order (the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} quadruple, including irreflexivity, totality and transitivity property tests)\revnote{208} / the settlement-window \texttt{openWindow/\allowbreak{}acceptCandidate/\allowbreak{}replaceCandidate/\allowbreak{}closeWindow} state machine (\hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}, including double-candidate supersession, baseline freezing when the forced or message queue is non-empty, and L1-reorg and lazy-close tests)\revnote{209} / landing / forced inclusion / the window \texttt{challenge} (\hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}) / the shared gas budget and overflow (\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}) / atomic rollback of \texttt{landed head} + state root + \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}consumed}/\texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed} + lag under an L1 reorg (\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 9) (the current prose relies heavily on review notes and cross-references, and the P1-level parent-block / fork-choice / lander interactions are easily missed by a reader); (b) the precise definition of the deterministic weighted sampling algorithm for the \texttt{lookahead} --- from r47 onwards \hyperref[sec:3.2]{\S3.2} gives a complete executable candidate operator (Python code using a capped weight prefix sum plus positioning by seed modulo total weight, verifiable by running \texttt{lookahead-\allowbreak{}model.py}), and this item closes once the owner has confirmed that operator; (c) terminologically distinguishing L2 acceptance finality from L1 finality \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} with different words (the conflated use of "final" makes the two-tier parent rule harder to reason about).
\end{enumerate}
\subsection*{Master parameter table \revnote{210}}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{Master parameter table }
\begin{center}\footnotesize

\clearpage\section*{Appendix A: Points of divergence from the owner's suggestions (pending the owner's confirmation)}\label{app:A}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Appendix A: Points of divergence from the owner's suggestions (pending the owner's confirmation)}
\markboth{}{}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{The lookahead randomness does not mix in a proof hash.} The owner suggested "L1 consensus-layer data + the hash of the most recent proof"; this document takes only the former. The reason (\hyperref[sec:3.2]{\S3.2}): a proof hash is entropy that the party producing the proof can re-grind at zero cost, so mixing it in hands partial control of the lookahead to the aggregator, which runs counter to the trust-minimization goal. The 1-bit-level bias residual of pure L1 beacon randomness is acceptable for the purposes of the lookahead.
\item \textbf{Parent linkage uses the block-header hash, not the parent's signature.} The owner put both options forward; this document makes the hash the consensus rule (it is structurally necessary and transitively commits to the entire history) and demotes carrying the parent's signature inside the child block to an optional evidence-packaging convention (\hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}).
\item \textbf{The residual risk from skipping is classified as deterrence-grade and explicitly accepted.} The signature chain eliminates intermediate deletion by the aggregator, but a skip by the adjacent builder is not falsifiable inside the protocol (\hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}). This document chooses to say so plainly and to handle it with publicity + reputation + a cap of one slot on the loss, rather than introducing an undecidable "but I did publish it" arbitration. If the owner wants elimination at the structural level, the known price is publishing a commitment to L1 every slot (fees revert to what they were before aggregation saved them) --- not recommended. (This sentence is precisely the cost identity cited in \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1} \revnote{218}: the fee for buying protocol-level commitments or DA for the pre-landing stage ≈ the fee this design saves by landing in aggregated batches --- buy the former and the latter's savings cease to exist.)
\item \textbf{The payment point for the base-fee share: the close, not provisional landing.} The owner suggested that "the base fee should be distributed directly to the aggregator at landing time"; this document chooses to account for it in the atomic commit at settlement-window close (\hyperref[sec:6.5]{\S6.5}). The reason: a provisional landing can be superseded inside the window by a heavier candidate (\hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}), so paying at landing would require designing a clawback mechanism for the superseded party --- that is a new state machine and a new attack surface. Paying at the close, by contrast, is part of the atomic commit at the close, requires zero additional machinery, and happens to implement exactly the "winner takes all" incentive (a superseded lander receives nothing, which maximizes the opportunity cost of landing a short chain). The only price is that the lander's cash flow is deferred by one \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} (≈20 minutes). If the owner insists on paying at landing, the complexity of a clawback state machine has to be accepted back into the design first.
\end{enumerate}
\clearpage\section*{Appendix B: Glossary}\label{app:B}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Appendix B: Glossary}
\markboth{}{}
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{0.30\linewidth}X}
\toprule
\textbf{Term} & \textbf{Definition} \\
\midrule
slot & The 1-second time unit of L2 \\
lookahead & The deterministic mapping from slot to builder address \\
builder & The preconfer that produces and signs a block according to the lookahead \\
aggregator & The service seat that packs, proves and lands \\
signature chain & The unlanded chain linked by parent\_hash and signed block by block \\
landing & A batch plus its proof going onto L1 as a candidate; at window close the heaviest becomes final \revnote{219} \\
batch & A stretch of contiguous signature chain + data + proof \\
gap & A slot with no legal block \\
skip & A builder's signed choice not to build on the immediately preceding block that does exist \\
fallback landing & Anyone landing, and claiming compensation, once the aggregator has timed out \\
forced-only block & A block that is exempt from the lookahead and contains only due forced entries \\
equivocation & Signing two different block headers for the same slot; slashed by direct verification on L1 \\
two-tier parent rule & The criterion is the gap: (i) a gap \texttt{≤ G\_\allowbreak{}max} (independent of the parent's landing status); (ii) no cap on the gap, but the parent must be an L1-final landed head \revnote{220} \\
recovery block & A normal or forced-only block that, during a stall, is built on an L1-final landed head under tier (ii) and reconnects to wall-clock time (\hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}) \\
benign stranding & When the parent tip of a recovery block is advanced, that block is voided and simply has to be rebuilt; no state is damaged (\hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}) \\
consume-and-discard & A forced or inbound entry that is illegal against the preceding state still advances the cursor but is not executed (\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}/\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, r9-2) \\
best-chain total order & Lexicographic order on the candidate's own quadruple \texttt{(\allowbreak{}lane,\allowbreak{} count,\allowbreak{} tip\_\allowbreak{}slot,\allowbreak{} tip\_\allowbreak{}hash)}; the lane is the class of the candidate's first block counted from the frozen baseline (content 1 > forced-only 0) and is inherited by the whole chain, so it cannot be whitewashed \revnote{221} \\
best-chain landing strategy & The lander lands the best chain under the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} total order --- not an obligation: landing a worse chain is superseded inside the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window by a heavier candidate and is self-defeating \revnote{222} \\
settlement window & Opens once the first candidate extending the window-final head has landed, and closes deterministically \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} L1 blocks later \revnote{223} \\
candidate batch & A landed batch that extends the window-final head and carries a validity proof; inside the window it can be superseded by one that is strictly heavier under the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} total order (\hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}) \\
window-final & The heaviest proven candidate at settlement-window close; withdrawals and bridging execute only from this state (\hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}/\hyperref[sec:6.1]{\S6.1}) \\
challenge / head-commitment & Removed. The accountability and commitment layers of v1.39/v1.40; independent review rounds 3/4 proved they cannot be mechanized without DA, and r41 replaced them with the settlement window \\
re-anchor episode & Removed. The recovery sub-protocol of v1.8–v1.30, deleted in its entirety by v1.31 direction B and replaced by the two-tier parent rule (\hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} is now settlement-window finality)\revnote{224} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

\clearpage\section*{Appendix C: Executable reference model of the settlement window (normative pseudocode + property verification)}\label{app:C}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Appendix C: Executable reference model of the settlement window (normative pseudocode + property verification)}
\markboth{}{}
\begin{designnote}
\textbf{The delivery of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 18, "the pre-implementation gate" \revnote{225}}. The normative pseudocode is that of this appendix and the body of \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}; the \textbf{executable version} is in \texttt{\texttt{settlement-\allowbreak{}window-\allowbreak{}model.py}} (dependency-free Python, runs directly), and the verification results are in \texttt{\texttt{settlement-\allowbreak{}window-\allowbreak{}RESULTS.md}}. \textbf{Discipline}: any change to the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} total order, the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window state machine or the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}/\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} cursor and gas rules must be mirrored in the model, the model re-run and RESULTS updated --- the three being out of sync counts as a specification defect (the same practice as the v15 model\_checker).
\end{designnote}

\textbf{Properties that have been verified \revnote{226}}:

\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{p{0.20\linewidth}p{0.22\linewidth}X}
\toprule
\textbf{Property} & \textbf{Content} & \textbf{Corresponding finding} \\
\midrule
P1 & The total-order key \texttt{(\allowbreak{}lane,\allowbreak{} count,\allowbreak{} tip\_\allowbreak{}slot,\allowbreak{} tip\_\allowbreak{}hash)} is irreflexive, total and transitive; the round-5 \texttt{A>B>C>A} cycle resolves to \texttt{B>C>A} & Severe 1 \\
P2 & The winner at the close is independent of the order in which candidates were submitted (verified over all permutations) & Corollary of severe 1 \\
