P3 & When the forced or message queue is non-empty: a provisional landing does not change what is canonical; a heavier candidate can be verified against the same frozen baseline and supersede it; the close commits the winner's outcome exactly once; the cursor is monotone with no double consumption & Severe 2 \\
P4 & Resistance to lane whitewashing: a 13-block chain whose first block is forced-only ranks below a 3-block content chain & Round 3, finding 3 \\
P5 & A lazy close does not change the winner; after the close, a candidate can only open the next window & Close boundary \\
P6 & Window state is a pure function of L1 history; after truncation by a shallow reorg, replay is consistent and reorged-out candidates disappear atomically & L1 reorg \\
P7 & Under the shared gas budget plus per-entry admission, a legal block exists for all 300 randomly generated reachable queue states (including non-genesis cursors); the maximum prefix under both constraints respects the caps & Rounds 4/5, gas deadlock \\
P8 & Under a saturating flood of the bridge queue, the ordinary forced-inclusion queue still consumes ≥ its guaranteed capacity every block (the \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} reservation takes effect and it is not starved) & r19-1; DeepSeek-on-v1.43 C2 \revnote{227} \\
P9 & The setter invariants are cross-checked between \textbf{independently declared deployment values} (\texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final ≥ prov+\allowbreak{}W\_\allowbreak{}settle\_\allowbreak{}max}, \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max ≥} the worst-case path, \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle ≥} proving + inclusion --- made non-vacuous in r46, since an assertion over derived expressions is always true and therefore has no discriminating power); causal ordering \texttt{anchor.L1\_\allowbreak{}time ≤ L2\_\allowbreak{}time(\allowbreak{}slot)} (an explicit time base, including the equality boundary) & \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}/\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}; introduced in r44, made non-vacuous in r46 \revnote{228} \\
P10 & Whether a slashing has taken effect is judged at the \textbf{L1 landing time of the candidate}: a candidate landing after it takes effect is rejected if it contains that signer, while one already landed before it takes effect is grandfathered & \hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3} r41; second half of the gate \revnote{229} \\
P11 & Fallback eligibility is snapshotted the moment the window opens on \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final > Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final} and is held until the window closes; a short provisional candidate that resets lag\_prov to zero does not revoke eligibility & \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} r42/r44, high-severity finding 2 \\
P12 & Enqueueing in mid-window: the queue is append-only and referenced by sequence number, and the baseline freezes only the cursor and state --- the outcome of earlier candidates is unchanged, a heavier candidate can consume the new entries deterministically, and the moment of enqueueing does not affect the verification result & \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}, clarified in r46 \revnote{230} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}

\textbf{Boundaries of the model (stated honestly)}: signature, proof and execution legality are placeholders (the model models precisely only the consensus objects this design adds: the total-order key, the window state machine, cursor arithmetic, gas shares and timing geometry). From r44 onwards, anchor geometry and causal ordering, the moment a slashing takes effect, and the fallback accounting snapshot are in the model (P9–P11); the storage layout and gas cost of the Solidity-level \texttt{acceptCandidate} entry point are an analytical (non-executable) review, see \texttt{\texttt{settlement-\allowbreak{}window-\allowbreak{}implementation-\allowbreak{}review.md}}. The base-fee share (\hyperref[sec:6.5]{\S6.5} \texttt{φ\_\allowbreak{}land})\revnote{231} is an economic action of the accounting at the close that changes neither window state nor cursor semantics, and is not yet in the model. \textbf{The final judgment is the owner plus a human safety review} --- the model and the review document are a gate, not a sign-off.

\clearpage\section*{Appendix D: Version History (Design Record)}\label{app:D}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Appendix D: Version History (Design Record)}
\markboth{}{}
\begin{designnote}
A version-by-version record of the findings and fixes of every round of adversarial review(newest first, oldest last). This is the complete argumentative trail of the design; it is non-normative, and where the main text conflicts with it, the main text governs.
\end{designnote}

\begin{designnote}
\textbf{Draft v1.50, 2026-08-27 --- a stale bold lead-in that contradicted option C is corrected.} The lead-in of the last bullet of \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} read "'which chain ought to be landed' is now a slashable obligation". That is a leftover from the v1.39/v1.40 challenge-layer era: those layers were falsified in rounds 3 and 4 of the independent review and deleted wholesale by option C (v1.41), at which point \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} and \hyperref[sec:10]{\S10} withdrew "landing the wrong chain is slashable" as well. The lead-in contradicted \textbf{the body of its own bullet} (which says L1 "does not adjudicate who ought to land what \textbackslash{}ldots\{\} no DA, no ex post adjudication"), the v1.47 characterization earlier in \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} (landing the longest visible chain is "the rational choice under economic incentives, not a slashable obligation"), and the slashing inventory of \hyperref[sec:10]{\S10} --- under the current design the only mechanically adjudicable slashable faults are double-signing (\hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3}) and landing-timeout strikes (\hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}). The lead-in now reads "L1 does not adjudicate 'which chain ought to be landed'; the settlement window selects it mechanically", in agreement with the bullet it heads. A pure consistency fix: no new rule, no parameter change, both executable models untouched.
\end{designnote}

\begin{designnote}
\textbf{Draft v1.49, 2026-08-27 --- the paper becomes an English edition, set in an academic serif face.} The owner's instruction was that the Chinese in the PDF looked poor and that the document should be converted to English with typography closer to an academic paper. Three changes: (1) \texttt{slot-\allowbreak{}chain-\allowbreak{}spec.en.md} is added, a complete English translation of the Chinese specification (translated in eight parallel slices against a shared glossary derived from \hyperref[app:B]{Appendix~B}; inline identifiers, §-references, list indentation and table structure were each verified against the source). The Chinese edition remains normative; the English file records the sha256 of the Chinese source in its header, and \texttt{build-\allowbreak{}pdf.py -\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}check} reports when the two have fallen out of step. (2) The PDF is now generated from the English edition: the document class moves from \texttt{ctexart} to \texttt{article}, the type is Palatino (\texttt{mathpazo}, text and math matched) with \texttt{microtype}, and the CJK font dependency is gone; the title page becomes a conventional \texttt{\textbackslash{}maketitle} plus \texttt{abstract}; 26 Unicode symbol mappings were added (≈ ≤ → ⊥ ✓ Δ φ and the rest routed through math mode) together with a full-width punctuation normalization pass. (3) The labels and captions of all 10 TikZ figures were translated and re-tuned for width, English running 1.6–2× wider than the Chinese it replaces; both pdflatex and xelatex verify zero overfull boxes, zero missing glyphs, and a widest picture of 145 mm. Specification semantics, parameters and both executable models are unchanged. \textbf{Draft v1.48, 2026-08-27 --- the typesetting pipeline switched to LaTeX: a single-column, academic-paper-style PDF, with review annotations externalized into \hyperref[app:E]{Appendix~E}.} The owner instructed: "convert it to LaTeX, single column, produce the PDF directly; the figures should be black, white and grey, in academic-paper style; every comment quoted from others and from the owner goes into an appendix at the very end". Three items landed: (1) a new \texttt{build-\allowbreak{}pdf.py}(Markdown → XeLaTeX + ctex → \texttt{slot-\allowbreak{}chain-\allowbreak{}spec.pdf}, 68 pages, A4 single column), including a title page, abstract, table of contents and headers/footers, with § and appendix references rendered as clickable cross-references; the body's section numbering is aligned with the document's own \hyperref[sec:0]{\S0}–\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}, and Appendices A–E are excluded from the numbering. (2) All 10 mermaid figures were redrawn as greyscale TikZ figures(\texttt{tex/\allowbreak{}figures.tex}, black/white/grey only, including one genuine sequence diagram), floated with the body text and captioned. (3) Review annotations externalized: roughly 310 provenance parentheticals in the body of the form "\revnote{232}" and "\revnote{233}" were extracted into endnotes \texttt{[n]} collected in the newly added \hyperref[app:E]{Appendix~E}; for parentheticals that put substantive content first and provenance second, only the provenance part was moved out, and normative content such as parameter values remains in the body. The HTML pipeline was deleted(\texttt{slot-\allowbreak{}chain-\allowbreak{}spec.html}, \texttt{build-\allowbreak{}html.py}); under \texttt{tex/\allowbreak{}} only the hand-written \texttt{figures.tex} is checked in, while \texttt{main.tex} and the compilation intermediates are excluded by \texttt{.gitignore}. Normative semantics, parameters and both models(21 + 6 assertions)are unchanged. \textbf{Draft v1.47, 2026-08-27 --- three instructions from the owner: characterizing the visibility of the longest chain + a base-fee revenue share as incentive, expressing the lookahead as code, and a further readability pass.} (1) Two additions to \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}: the visibility assumption and the characterization "economic only, not compulsory" --- the protocol cannot force a lander to land the longest signature chain(the builder at the chain tip can withhold its signature and thereby deprive others of visibility, which is not a fault), and landing a shorter chain carries only economic consequences; the node-local chain-selection rule is stated explicitly as the existing four-tuple total order(including the equivocation-fork case --- the equivocation is slashed, but the two chains remain comparable, so view convergence is not broken); and the trade-off "the sum of fees does not enter the total order" is recorded(fees can be self-paid to inflate volume, so letting them into the canonicality criterion would amount to letting capital buy out the honest majority --- fees enter only the reward). \hyperref[sec:6.5]{\S6.5} adds the base-fee revenue share: the sum of all base fees of the candidate chain × \texttt{φ\_\allowbreak{}land} is credited to the winner's beneficiary in the atomic commit at window close; the revenue-share base is monotonic in chain length and a superseded candidate receives nothing; the payment point is taken to be the close rather than provisional landing(which avoids a clawback state machine), the deviation from the owner's proposal is recorded in Appendix A-4, and \texttt{φ\_\allowbreak{}land} enters the parameter table. (2) \hyperref[sec:3.2]{\S3.2} embeds the complete executable Python for the lookahead(with step-by-step comments), and a new \texttt{lookahead-\allowbreak{}model.py}(6 properties: pure function/window alignment/snapshot-finality geometry/w\_max capping and proportionality to weight); the sampling operator(capped prefix sums + seed-modulo positioning)is the candidate form for \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}-18(b), and that item closes once the owner confirms it. (3) Readability: about 670 mid-sentence bold spans were removed document-wide(roughly 300 line-initial label-style bold spans were kept); the HTML generator was fixed for nested-list parsing(tab\_length=2 + supplying the missing blank line before a list that follows a paragraph), § and appendix references now become anchor links automatically(700+ of them), and the bold weight was reduced to 600. \textbf{Draft v1.46, 2026-08-26 --- the DeepSeek-on-v1.45 batch: restoring the model's discriminating power + recalibrating one genuine numerical contradiction.} \textbf{(W1) Making P9a non-vacuous --- and it immediately caught the numerical slack in r44}: the model had previously defined \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}ANCHOR\_\allowbreak{}MAX} as "the worst-case path + 5" and then asserted "the worst-case path ≤ D\_ANCHOR\_MAX", which is trivially true and has no discriminating power. Fix: all timing parameters were changed to \textbf{independently declared deployment-value literals}(copied from the parameter table), and P9a cross-checks them with the inequality. Once non-vacuous it failed immediately --- r44's initial \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final} of 8 epoch(51.2 min)is below its own formula \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov +\allowbreak{} W\_\allowbreak{}settle\_\allowbreak{}max} = 128 + 150 L1 slot(55.6 min). Recalibration: the initial \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final} = 9 epoch = 288 L1 slot ≈ 57.6 min; and correspondingly the initial estimate of \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} goes 380 → ≈420 L1 slot(≈84 minutes)(\hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2}/\hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}/\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}/\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}/the parameter table/two figures synchronized). (W2) Clarification of the frozen-baseline semantics + P12: what is frozen is [the cursors and the state], not the queue contents --- the queue is append-only, referenced by sequence number, and immutable in content, so an enqueue in mid-window is visible and determinate to any later candidate whose coverage reaches it; in implementation the queue commitment must be per-entry/append-only (a hash chain or an MMR), and a single mutable queue root taken at landing time must not be used as a public input(a new clause in \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} + a synchronized \texttt{baseCommit} section in the pre-implementation review document). The model adds P12a/P12b(a mid-window enqueue does not change the outcome for earlier candidates; the moment of enqueue does not affect the verification result), and all 21 assertions pass. \textbf{(W3) Regeneration discipline}: the HTML generator of the time gained a \texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}check} mode(regenerate and diff, exiting non-zero if dirty)\revnote{234}, and the README states the convention "edit the md → regenerate + check"; the monorepo CI workflow is out of scope for this docs branch and is listed as repository-level follow-up at merge time. (Suggestion) P9b is strengthened to check \texttt{anchor.L1\_\allowbreak{}timestamp ≤ L2\_\allowbreak{}timestamp(\allowbreak{}slot)} against an explicit time-base function (including the equality boundary); \texttt{build-\allowbreak{}html.py} should manage files with with and record its dependency versions. \textbf{Draft v1.45, 2026-08-26 --- readability revision\revnote{235}.} Round 1(structure): roughly 400 lines of version history were moved from the top of the document into this appendix, and a "how to read this document" guide(including the reading conventions for review parentheticals)plus a table of contents were added before the main text. Round 2(typography): half-width commas/semicolons/colons inside Chinese-language context were unified to full-width(about 630 of them; code blocks and inline code untouched). Rounds 3/4(figures): 10 mermaid figures were added to the body --- \hyperref[sec:0]{\S0} the end-to-end path and the gap timeline, \hyperref[sec:3.2]{\S3.2} the lookahead snapshot geometry, \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} the decision flow of the two-tier parent rule, \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} the settlement-window lifecycle, \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2} the four-level certainty ladder and the two thresholds, \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} the causal chain of fallback and strikes, \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} the stall-recovery timing, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} maximum-prefix consumption over the two queues, \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} the anchor freshness geometry --- all in diagram-as-code form(rendered natively by GitHub, not pasted images), and each one validated by rendering it through mermaid-cli. The \hyperref[sec:0]{\S0} abstract sentences were split and polished, and references in the body to the old "change log" now point to this appendix. Round 5(final read): a full re-read of the document, residual patching, and the addition of an HTML edition \texttt{slot-\allowbreak{}chain-\allowbreak{}spec.html}\revnote{236}. Normative semantics, parameters and the model(19 assertions)are all unchanged. \textbf{Draft v1.44, 2026-08-26 --- fixes for the DeepSeek-on-v1.43 batch + delivery of the second half of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 18, "the gate before implementation".} \textbf{(C1, high) Splitting the threshold: \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov} / \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final}} --- r42 switched the quantity used to decide fallback/strikes to \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final} but kept the old threshold \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} (4 epoch ≈25.6 min); under window finality the steady state \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final ≈ lag\_\allowbreak{}prov +\allowbreak{} W\_\allowbreak{}settle ≈ 35–40 min} is by itself already above the old threshold → in a healthy system the fallback window would be permanently open and a diligent aggregator would accumulate strikes continuously until terminated. Fix: each of the two lags gets its own threshold, \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final = Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov +\allowbreak{} W\_\allowbreak{}settle\_\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} margin} (initially ≈8 epoch ≈51.2 min), and all penalty decisions in \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2}/\hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} now use it; \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} adds a terminological convention (a bare \texttt{lag}/\texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} reads as final, clearing W3); ripple: the lag term of the \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} formula is likewise raised to \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}final} (the fallback opens its window on lag\_final, so the freshness window must cover the later authorization point; the initial estimate goes 250→380 L1 slot). (C2) The bridge reservation was missing from the model --- \texttt{settlement-\allowbreak{}window-\allowbreak{}model.py} used to give the whole of \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} to the bridge queue, omitting the \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} reservation of r19-1; fixed, plus a new P8 (the ordinary queue is not starved under a saturating bridge flood). (W2) Normalizing close-before-accept --- the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} pseudocode now explicitly requires \texttt{L1.now < close\_\allowbreak{}at} before a candidate can be admitted, and at the same height the close happens before admission (consistent with the model's replay semantics). \textbf{(W1) The \hyperref[app:B]{Appendix~B} entry "best-chain total order" was updated to the r42 four-tuple.} Second half of the gate delivered: the model adds P9 (anchor geometry/causal ordering), P10 (slashing keyed to the candidate's landing point) and P11 (the fallback-eligibility snapshot), and all 19 assertions pass; plus a new \texttt{settlement-\allowbreak{}window-\allowbreak{}implementation-\allowbreak{}review.md} (a Solidity-level storage/gas analysis of \texttt{acceptCandidate}, the Inbox integration path, and item-by-item disposition of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 18 (a)/(b)/(c); the final judgment = the owner + a human security review). \textbf{Sections touched}: \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}, \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2}, \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}, \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} (items 3/15/18, the parameter table), Appendices B/C, the model and the new document. \textbf{Draft v1.43, 2026-08-26 --- delivery of the first half of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 18, "the gate before implementation": an executable reference model of the settlement window + property verification(\hyperref[app:C]{Appendix~C}).} New files \texttt{settlement-\allowbreak{}window-\allowbreak{}model.py}(runnable with zero dependencies)and \texttt{settlement-\allowbreak{}window-\allowbreak{}RESULTS.md}: the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} total-order key, the \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window state machine(frozen baseline/candidate versioning/close-time commit/lazy close/L1 replay)and the two-constraint cursor arithmetic of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}/\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} are written out as an executable model, and all 14 assertions P1–P7 pass --- including every invariant of the two blocking findings of round 5(dissolving the A/B/C cycle, independence from submission order, provisional does not change canonical, no double consumption), lane-whitewash resistance, lazy close, the L1-reorg pure function, and the absence of gas deadlock across 300 randomized queue states. Discipline: any change to the rules must be mirrored in the model and re-run \revnote{237}. The second half of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 18(the pre-implementation review: items the model does not cover + the Solidity-level entry points)remains open and must be completed before implementation begins. Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 18, \hyperref[app:C]{Appendix~C}(new), and two new files. \textbf{Draft v1.42, 2026-08-26 --- fixes from independent review round 5/5(the final round): closing the mathematics and the state machine of the settlement window.} Conclusion of the final round: option C is the right direction(the fatal flaw of the two-layer committed-head/challenge design is confirmed eliminated), but v1.41's execution had two blocking problems: \textbf{(severe 1) The total order is not transitive} --- the old rule "look at the first differing block of the two chains to determine the lane" is a pairwise criterion, so an \texttt{A>B>C>A} cycle can be constructed and the winner at the close drifts with submission order. Fix: the lane becomes an invariant scalar of the candidate itself(= the class of the candidate's first block counting from \texttt{F}, inherited by the whole chain), and the comparison is the lexicographic order on the four-tuple \texttt{(\allowbreak{}lane,\allowbreak{} count,\allowbreak{} tip\_\allowbreak{}slot,\allowbreak{} tip\_\allowbreak{}hash)} --- a total order by construction, and still whitewash-resistant(\hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}). (severe 2) A provisional candidate advancing the canonical cursor --- the first candidate to land immediately advanced \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}consumed}/the state, so a second, heavier candidate's starting cursor no longer matched and it could not compete, reinstating "first to land wins forever". Fix: \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} adds the \textbf{frozen baseline + candidate versioning + close-time commit} state machine(opening the window freezes \texttt{base=(\allowbreak{}F,\allowbreak{}stateRoot,\allowbreak{}F\_\allowbreak{}consumed,\allowbreak{}m\_\allowbreak{}consumed)}, all candidates are verified against that same base and each stores its own end tuple, and the close commits only the winner's end tuple in a single atomic write; the \texttt{openWindow/\allowbreak{}acceptCandidate/\allowbreak{}closeWindow} pseudocode enters the body; it is honestly acknowledged that this is a small window state machine)\revnote{238}, the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} cursor rules are re-read accordingly, and the leftover text "first to land is canonical" in \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} is deleted. (high 1) The bounded-inclusion assumption is made explicit --- a fixed \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle}/\texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} deadline does not entail unconditional robustness against temporary censorship; \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1} explicitly adds the \texttt{T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max} assumption + the setter relations, and the affected statements are made conditional. (high 2) Fallback accounting decides on \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final} and keeps a snapshot until the close --- this prevents a short candidate from clearing the provisional lag and cutting off the correction reward; the reward is paid to the winner and to every strict improver. \textbf{(high 3) The \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} formula uniformly gains \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} in all three places}(without it, a legal parameter set necessarily strands honest tails). \textbf{(medium 1) Splitting \texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}prov}(service)/\texttt{lag\_\allowbreak{}final}(safety)}, with the revocable exposure honestly restated as \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag +\allowbreak{} W\_\allowbreak{}settle (\allowbreak{}wall clock) +\allowbreak{} fallback response}, and \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} given a consensus upper bound. (medium 2) \texttt{submission\_\allowbreak{}slot := L2\_\allowbreak{}slot(\allowbreak{}timestamp of the enqueuing L1 block)} is made unique(under a huge stall the alternative reading "take the L2 head" would fork). Consistency: a tier (ii) parent must be both window-final and L1-final(waiting bound \texttt{max(\allowbreak{}W\_\allowbreak{}settle,\allowbreak{}F\_\allowbreak{}l1)+\allowbreak{}D\_\allowbreak{}anchor}), the \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced} summary gains the tier (i) continuation case, the \hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3} "no collateral damage" wording is changed to acknowledge the cascade exposure of the successors of an equivocation, and the \texttt{δ\_\allowbreak{}land} table row/the challenge leftovers/the glossary are cleaned up. Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}, \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}, \hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3}, \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}, \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}, \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}, \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2}, \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}, \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}, \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}, \hyperref[app:B]{Appendix~B}. \textbf{Draft v1.41, 2026-08-26 --- the owner's decision, option C: settlement-window finality, wholly replacing the challenge/commitment layer + fixes from independent review round 4.} Round 4 proved that v1.40's "lightweight commitment" still does not hold: a chain-head commitment cannot prove that the block body is available(commit the head then withhold the body → the honest lander lands the only chain it can land → the attacker reveals the body afterwards and challenges → the honest party is wrongly slashed / everyone stops serving), a single commitment ledger can be permanently poisoned by one deliberately-worse landing, and bounded censorship still penetrates the commitment window --- repairing it = full DA + a generational commitment history + a challenge/deposit state machine = the entire complexity of the episode design flowing back in. On this basis the owner changed the root\revnote{239}: \textbf{the entire challenge/commitment layer is deleted\revnote{240}, and the new \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} = settlement-window finality} --- landing is no longer "accepted means final"; instead the heaviest proven candidate within a window \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle} becomes final at the close; a better chain wins outright by landing into the window carrying its own proof, and no after-the-fact L1 adjudication of "should have landed but did not" is required. Comparison takes place only among candidates that have all landed and are all proven, hence: the block-body availability problem does not exist(without producing the body you cannot form a candidate); there is no cross-window state and no poisoning; close-time sniping is benign(heavier = more genuine signed blocks = a more complete tail finalized); a fixed window suffices, with no chess clock. Landing a worse chain changes from "a slashable fault" to self-defeating(being overwritten wastes the cost)--- \hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}/\hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}/\hyperref[sec:10]{\S10} correspondingly withdraw "landing the wrong chain is slashable". The cost (stated explicitly): finality + one \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle}(≈20 minutes, with withdrawals executed from window-final); the setter \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}settle ≥ P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} margin}. Solved along the way: the stale-anchor tail deadlock is now resolved by parameter geometry (\texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max ≥ D\_\allowbreak{}anchor +\allowbreak{} Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag +\allowbreak{} P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} margin} ≈ 250 L1 slot, \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}); the grandfathering criterion for slashing = the L1 landing point of the candidate batch(unforgeable, \hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3}, with \texttt{δ\_\allowbreak{}land} deleted); and lag is measured on the best provisional candidate(so liveness is not dragged down by the window, \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2}/\hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}). \textbf{Independent bugs from round 4 fixed in the same pass}: \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} is purged of the leftover "exactly min(entry count)" text and unified on the two-constraint longest prefix, with the backlog bound changed to \texttt{max(\allowbreak{}entry count/\allowbreak{}C\_\allowbreak{}anchor,\allowbreak{} gas/\allowbreak{}share)}(severe 3); \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike} units unified to L2 seconds + an L1 slot conversion(medium 3); \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force} is re-verified per block and a lane continuation does not inherit maturity eligibility(medium 1); the recovery bound gains a \texttt{+\allowbreak{}D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} term(medium 2); \texttt{final\_\allowbreak{}ref} enters the block-header tuple and \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} (consistency 1); the three-tier depths of \hyperref[sec:10]{\S10} are made conditional; and the \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} wording is corrected(consistency 7). Six rounds of self-review have been folded into the body of \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}(sniping is benign/fixed window/parameter invariants/L1 pure function/anti-spam/three residual risks). Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}, \hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3}, \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}, \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}, \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}, \hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}, \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}(rewritten as a whole), \hyperref[sec:6.1]{\S6.1}, \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2}, \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}, \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}, \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}, \hyperref[sec:10]{\S10}, \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}, \hyperref[app:B]{Appendix~B}(\hyperref[sec:5.7]{\S5.7} deleted). \textbf{Draft v1.40, 2026-08-25 --- the owner's decision, option B: add a lightweight [chain-head commitment layer] so that challenges become mechanically enforceable + fixes from independent review round 3.} Round 3 proved that v1.39's challenge(pure signature verification)can neither mechanically prove that a competing chain "existed before the landing"(a block's self-reported slot can be forged by signing after the fact)nor prove "execution validity" --- so it either wrongly slashes honest landers, or it requires DA/commitments/proofs in order to stand up. On this basis the owner chose option B: a new \hyperref[sec:5.7]{\S5.7} chain-head commitment accumulator --- permissionless and bonded(\texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}commit}), in which anyone submits the chain head + the weight of a better legal signature chain(tens of bytes, no block body, no ZK)to L1 and L1 stamps it with an unforgeable timestamp; this is partial DA(chain heads only), and its cost is explicitly accepted in exchange for "challenges being mechanically decidable". The \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} challenge is re-anchored on the commitment timestamp: "the competing chain existed before the landing" is proven by the L1 commitment time(closing off after-the-fact signing)\revnote{241}, the divergence point must lie within the interval \texttt{B} has already landed\revnote{242}, and an invalid chain is disproven by the lander's validity proof → the committer is slashed \texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}commit}(closing off impersonation with invalid blocks)\revnote{243}; the property that it only slashes and never changes finality is unchanged. The commitment layer simultaneously repairs: \textbf{finding 1, the stale-anchor tail deadlock} --- the anchor freshness of a committed block is judged by [the commitment time] rather than the landing time, so it can still land even if landing is withheld for a long time(\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}); finding 5, the \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} boundary pre-signing loop --- a tier-(ii) block header gains \texttt{final\_\allowbreak{}ref} to prove that the parent was already final at signing time, rejecting pre-signing(\hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}); finding 7, slashing backfill --- grandfathering now uses the commitment timestamp, made precise as "did it truly exist before the effective moment" (\hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3}). Independently fixed bugs: finding 3, lane whitewashing --- the lane is fixed at the first block after the fork and inherited by the whole branch, so a single ordinary block cannot whitewash a forced-only scaffold(\hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}); finding 4, the gas-prefix contradiction --- unified as "the longest FIFO prefix whose entry count ≤ the cap and whose cumulative gas ≤ the share", deleting "exactly min(entry count)"(\hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}/\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}); finding 8, integer threshold-riding --- \texttt{late\_\allowbreak{}units = max(\allowbreak{}0,\allowbreak{} preLag−(\allowbreak{}Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag−1))}, with the minimum non-zero penalty at equality(\hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}); finding 9, \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force} --- folded into \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced} predicate 4(an any-key block must be overdue by ≥ \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force}, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}). Consistency: the old standalone statement "block count takes priority" in \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} is deleted and folded into the total order; the \hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}/\hyperref[sec:10]{\S10} conclusions are taken up as the third class of slashable fault in \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}; the \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} D\_anchor formula is corrected; and "never expires" is scoped to the gap dimension. \textbf{Stated residual risk(\hyperref[sec:5.7]{\S5.7})}: the authenticity of \texttt{HC} depends on "≥1 honest committer"; a commitment proves only that the head exists, not that the block body is available(a body-withholding chain can still contest); this layer is the minimal reflux of the chain-head commitment/DA that was originally deleted to save cost, and option B accepts it explicitly. Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}, \hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3}, \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}, \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}, \hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}, \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}, \hyperref[sec:5.7]{\S5.7}(new), \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}, \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}, \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, \hyperref[sec:10]{\S10}, \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}, \hyperref[app:B]{Appendix~B}. \textbf{Draft v1.39, 2026-08-25 --- the owner's decision to introduce a "challenge-and-slash" layer + fixes for the severe findings of independent review rounds 1/2.} A deep independent AI review\revnote{244}proved that under "landers are entirely untrusted + no protocol-level DA per \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}", pure off-chain fork choice cannot stop a malicious lander from skipping a healthy tail and landing a worse chain, nor stop a single aggregator from resetting repeatedly at zero penalty; and that a mechanical guarantee against malicious landers is undecidable without DA. On this basis the owner decided to add an accountability layer that "only slashes, never changes the chain": \textbf{a new \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}, the obligation to land the best chain + challenge-and-slash} --- the lander shall land the "best chain"(the new total order of \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}: content lane > forced-only lane; then block count, then higher slot, then the hash of the first differing child block)among those that have propagated and settled; if it skips a better settled chain, anyone may submit evidence to L1 within the accountability window \texttt{W\_\allowbreak{}chal} (pure signature verification, the evidence being a chain that was signed and in existence long beforehand, with no clock race)→ the landing bond \texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}land} is confiscated and the reporter rewarded. The key point: a challenge only slashes; it never rolls back and never changes finality (landing is final, as determined by the proving system)--- which is fundamentally different from the "re-anchoring episode" deleted before v1.30(which changed canonicality, required rollback and raced the proving latency), and so avoids the old fatal flaw. On this basis the severe findings of the independent review are fixed: (1) an honest lander discarding a landable healthy long tail under the old "recovery takes priority" instruction --- \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} is changed to "the best chain includes the frozen long tail; land it first, then recover from its final tip, without losing the tail", with \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} synchronized\revnote{245}; (2) a single aggregator resetting repeatedly at zero penalty --- every landing of a worse chain is slashable under \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}, and \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} adds lag debt settled as of the moment before acceptance, blocking integer threshold-riding; (3) a heavier any-key forced chain overwriting a content tail --- \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}'s lane priority rules it out directly, and it is slashable. Fixed in the same pass, the high-severity/consistency items of the independent review: the combined-gas chain stall(\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} adds a shared block-level gas budget + a per-message gas cap + determinism priority/overflow/watermark); the \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} formula omitting \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor}(\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} adds \texttt{≥ D\_\allowbreak{}anchor +\allowbreak{} P\_\allowbreak{}prove,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} T\_\allowbreak{}include,\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} margin}); a slashed old key backfilling historical slots(\hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3} adds a second gate, "keyed to the batch acceptance point + a \texttt{δ\_\allowbreak{}land} grace period"); leftover \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} text, \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed} changed to a global message sequence number, the "tier (i) unlanded parent" wording, a single expiry predicate for \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force/\allowbreak{}F\_\allowbreak{}delay}, and updates to the parameter table and the glossary. Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:4.3]{\S4.3}, \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}, \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}(new), \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3}, \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}, \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}, \hyperref[sec:10]{\S10}, \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}, \hyperref[app:B]{Appendix~B}. Stated residual risk(\hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6}): the challenge is economic deterrence, not a safety guarantee(submitting the evidence must be economically viable); an honest but eclipsed lander may be wrongly slashed(mitigated by setting \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}prop} low); and since finality is unchanged, a skipped preconfirmation means "delay, not recovery". All are items explicitly accepted under the no-DA model of \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}. \textbf{Draft v1.38, 2026-08-25}: Codex 1×P1 + DeepSeek 1 Critical + 4 warnings, focused on anchor freshness/censorship resistance/interactions with forced-only blocks: (Codex P1) anchor freshness can make a recovery block expire --- tier (ii) eliminates "expiry by gap", but the \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} freshness rule still applies, so a recovery block must be proven and landed within the freshness window of its anchor. Fix: \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} scopes "never expires" to "does not expire because of the gap", and a new setter invariant \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max ≥ proving latency +\allowbreak{} worst-\allowbreak{}case landing delay} is added(\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}); the crucial difference is that \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} is a freely settable parameter, not the 64-second window that the old \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}ra} pinned down structurally. (DeepSeek Critical) The last-block-of-the-batch freshness shortcut does not hold --- the anchor is only monotonically non-decreasing, so a fresh last block does not imply that earlier blocks are fresh, and checking only the last block would let earlier blocks discharge forced-inclusion obligations against a stale L1 view. Fix: \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} now requires freshness to be checked block by block, and the "or the last block of the batch" shortcut is deleted. (DeepSeek W1) The censorship-resistance bound is \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max}, not \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} alone --- correcting the over-strong claim "consuming fewer entries makes the block illegal": the freshness lower bound forces the anchor frontier to advance as landing proceeds, so the worst-case message delay is ≈ \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} +\texttt{backlog/\allowbreak{}C\_\allowbreak{}anchor}(\hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}). (DeepSeek W2) A tie between a forced-only block and a lookahead block at the same slot/parent --- an identical fork point makes the hash tie-break ineffective. Fix: \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} adds "at the same slot/parent a lookahead block takes priority over a forced-only block, unless the ordinary tail is already frozen". \textbf{(DeepSeek W3) "final" is overloaded} --- \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} is annotated \texttt{L1-\allowbreak{}final} versus \hyperref[sec:6.1]{\S6.1} \texttt{landed-\allowbreak{}final}(a document-wide unification is listed as 18(c)). (DeepSeek W4) The bounds depend on undetermined parameters --- the concrete bounds in \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}/\hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} are marked provisional(\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}). Also fixed: the nested backticks in the Markdown of \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced} predicate 3. Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}, \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}, \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}, \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}. \textbf{Draft v1.37, 2026-08-25}: Codex(14:43)one P1 --- a forced-only block cannot be continued after it lands, so the \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} rate limit contradicts the per-block draining at \texttt{backlog/\allowbreak{}C\_\allowbreak{}force} of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}. The original \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced} predicate 2 required every forced-only block to use tier (ii)(a final landed parent): under a total cartel the first forced-only block becomes the canonical head once landed but is not yet final, and the second can be built neither on it(\texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} not yet reached)nor on the old final head(a landing must extend the current head)→ every block must wait for \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} finalization, and per-block draining comes to nothing. This is exactly the chaining that the v1.35 finding-5 note "the same applies to forced-only continuation" implicitly assumed but that predicate 2 forbade. Fix: \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced} predicate 2 is relaxed to a choice of two --- (re-rooting) tier (ii) on a final landed head[the start of the chain, or re-rooting after a freeze], or (same-lane continuation) tier (i) chaining onto the previous forced-only block(the parent must satisfy \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced}, with gap ≤ \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max}). Forced-only blocks can therefore chain and drain at a density of \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} per block even under a cartel; restricting the parent to be a forced-only block → an any-key holder cannot inject into an honest lookahead tail via tier (i)(the two lanes are separated by "whether the parent satisfies \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced}", and a malicious forced-only block remains constrained by the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} recovery priority with respect to a healthy content tail). Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced}. \textbf{Draft v1.36, 2026-08-25}: Codex third batch(14:38)one P1(line 598)--- the L1→L2 causal-ordering invariant that the anchor must not be later than the block's own slot time. \hyperref[sec:6.1]{\S6.1} rule 3 only blocks future slots and \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} only blocks an anchor that is too old; neither blocks "an old slot paired with a new anchor": a builder can withhold an old slot, afterwards pick a fresh anchor that already contains a new L1→L2 message, and sign a tier (i) continuation that consumes that message into an L2 block whose slot timestamp precedes the L1 block the message came from, with a Byzantine aggregator racing it into a landing → breaking L1→L2 causal ordering and distorting the bridge-deadline semantics that are based on \texttt{block.timestamp}. Fix: \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} adds the invariant \texttt{anchor.L1\_\allowbreak{}timestamp ≤ L2 timestamp(\allowbreak{}slot)}(applying uniformly to ordinary blocks and forced-only blocks, with \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 5 and \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced} predicate 5 synchronized). Recovery is unaffected(a recovery block's anchor is at depth ≥ \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} ≈6.4 minutes back and its timestamp is always ≤ the wall-clock slot time, so it satisfies the invariant naturally); only the malformed "old slot + new anchor" combination is rejected. \textbf{Sections touched}: \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}.(The two items at line 855/1036 in the same batch = resends, against the new head)\revnote{246} \textbf{Draft v1.35, 2026-08-25}: Codex second batch(14:31)two P1s, both refinements that tighten the claims of v1.34(rather than overturning them): (P1 line 916) "one hop" refers to re-rooting; continued progress still requires the ordinary liveness floor --- the recovery block \texttt{R} is an unlanded P2P block, and producing a proof and landing it takes 10–15 minutes, during which ordering continues over P2P on top of \texttt{R}; "a recovery block never expires" refers precisely to the landing legality of \texttt{R}(tier (ii) has no gap cap), not to \texttt{R} being extendable without maintenance. The exact condition for recovery liveness is therefore "lookahead builders keep returning at a density of ≥1/\texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max}"(= the ordinary \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} liveness floor, not something specific to recovery); a single sparse builder will cause \texttt{R} to freeze → a retry \texttt{R'} on the same old landed head, which is benign and loses no ground, and recovery follows as soon as participation returns to the floor. The wording of \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}/\hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} is tightened. (P1 line 1102) A unilateral Byzantine aggregator can isolate a branch that it sources itself --- the any-key exemption lets a malicious aggregator itself(by enqueuing forced entries of its own + waiting \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}delay})manufacture a tier-(ii) forced-only branch and land it in person, so the "collusion" in \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}'s "requires the lander to collude" can collapse to a single untrusted aggregator acting alone, with no need to suborn a lookahead builder and no slashable equivocation. The boundaries are unchanged: it is kept out of a healthy tail by the \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} recovery priority (it is followed only when the tail is frozen), its depth is still ≤ the unlanded tail, it reaches only tier-2 revocable preconfirmations, and its content is forced-only (discretionary transactions return to the mempool). Corrected wording: the frequency of the forced-only path is not constrained by "the number of malicious lookahead slots", and the harm is bounded by the three limits above; this is explicitly folded into the untrusted-aggregator residual risk of \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}/\hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}. Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}, \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}, \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}. \textbf{Draft v1.34, 2026-08-25}: reviews by Codex and DeepSeek of the new direction-B design\revnote{247}--- four genuinely new holes(not old mechanisms resurfacing)have been fixed: \textbf{(P1) Recovery/fork-choice deadlock\revnote{248}}: after a true stall, the long tail from before the stall has the greater block count yet is frozen and cannot be extended, so \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}'s "most blocks" rule would make honest nodes cling to it and starve the one-block recovery block, deadlocking recovery against fork choice. Fix: \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} adds recovery priority --- a branch whose head lags the wall clock by more than \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} is a [frozen dead branch] and yields to a tier-(ii) recovery block built on the final landed head; the yielding criterion is identical to the recovery trigger criterion, so it fires only on a true stall and never isolates a healthy tail(whose head is always within \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} of the wall clock). \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} references it accordingly. (P1) \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed} has only an upper bound and no lower bound\revnote{249}: a pure upper bound lets a builder keep processing 0 L1→L2 messages while still using a fresh anchor, censoring inbound messages indefinitely and invalidating the \texttt{backlog/\allowbreak{}C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} catch-up bound. Fix: \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} adds mandatory maximum-prefix consumption \texttt{min(\allowbreak{}C\_\allowbreak{}anchor,\allowbreak{} pending)}(a public FIFO queue + a queue root + a new legality obligation in \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 5, exactly the same shape as the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} forced-inclusion queue). (P1) \texttt{≤ Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} is conditional\revnote{250}: the tail is ≤ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} only if the fallback lands on time; if the fallback stalls or its landing is censored on L1 the tail grows without bound, and a single tier-(ii) block can isolate a tail far longer than \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}. Fix: \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}/\hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4} honestly note that this upper bound is conditional on the same condition as the \hyperref[sec:6.3]{\S6.3} Byzantine liveness bound, and no unredeemable bound is claimed. (P2) The admission predicate for forced-only blocks\revnote{251}: \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}'s reference to the deleted "three conditions" is replaced by the precise predicate \texttt{P\_\allowbreak{}forced} of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}(any key + a tier-(ii) final parent + \texttt{slot ≤ wall clock} + a non-empty forced prefix up to capacity + maximum message consumption), together with an explanation that under any-key issuance slot uniqueness is resolved by fork choice + atomic landing rather than by equivocation slashing. The rest: \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} adds the \texttt{anchor} field to the block header\revnote{252}; \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} adds forced-carrier migration (16), genesis bootstrap (17), and formal pseudocode/the lookahead sampling algorithm/terminological distinctions (18). DeepSeek's "landed but not final" Critical and its "\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} three conditions" warning on v1.32 had already been fixed in v1.33\revnote{253}. Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}, \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}, \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}, \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}, \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}, \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}. \textbf{Draft v1.33, 2026-08-25}: DeepSeek's review of v1.32(2 Critical + 4 warnings + 3 suggestions, all directed at the new direction-B design rather than old mechanisms resurfacing)--- adopted, and converging. Key point 1\revnote{254}: the criterion of the two-tier parent rule was misread as "the landing status of the parent block", which made a parent that is "landed but not final" appear to fall into no tier at all and made the first block of every batch look illegal. Correction: the criterion is explicitly the gap \texttt{s − parent.slot}, evaluated on the structure of the signature chain and identical in all three places(signing/proving/landing), and it does not depend on the drifting landing status --- tier (i) is a bounded gap(independent of the parent's landing status; normal block production, including the first block after a landing, always takes this tier), and tier (ii) is an unbounded gap but the parent must have landed final. "Landed but not final" therefore leaves no gap in the coverage(restated in \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}/\hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}). Key point 2\revnote{255}: the "one hop" of one-hop recovery elided the \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} transient --- if the stall begins shortly after a landing and the current landed tip has not yet reached \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1}, the recovery block must wait for the tip to become final(≤ \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1})before it can land(otherwise it would fork away the newer tip and \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} would reject it). Correction: the recovery bound is written out in full as max(0, \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} − how long the tip has existed) + one round of proving, and in steady state a catastrophic stall degenerates to a pure single hop(\hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}/\hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}/\hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}/\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} updated). Warnings: \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 1's stale reference to "the three conditions of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}" is changed to "a forced-only block as defined in \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}"(W1); the \hyperref[sec:10]{\S10} slashing table is completed with the tier (ii) path of isolating a single block via the landed head, with the same bound across all three tiers(W2); the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} prose on cursors/snapshots gains a "net rule" sentence clarifying that the per-block anchor is the sole expiry criterion and that \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}consumed} only records consumption progress(W3); and the tier (ii) residual risk of \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4} gains the premise "the current tip is already final"(W4). Suggestion 3(marking deleted parameters as historical)is partly kept: the change-history notes for \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall}/\texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}base} and others are retained to preserve review provenance, and all are clearly labelled "deleted". Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}, \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1}, \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}, \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}, \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9}, \hyperref[sec:10]{\S10}. \textbf{Draft v1.32, 2026-08-25}: Codex's first review of v1.31, one P1\revnote{256}--- \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} was bound to the wrong object: v1.31 applied it to the per-block advance of the anchor reference, which conflicts with the \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} anchor freshness rule, so under a long stall (> \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor +\allowbreak{} D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max}) a recovery block's anchor could only advance \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} from the stale parent anchor, would still be stale, and would fail the freshness check --- failing precisely on the long stall it was meant to support. Fix: \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} is rebound to a separate L1→L2 message-processing cursor \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed}(≤ \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor} messages per block), while the anchor reference itself remains monotonically non-decreasing and must be fresh, with no per-block cap. A recovery block therefore references a fresh anchor(one-hop recovery of ordering, preserving C1), and a flood of inbound messages is processed block by block under \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed}(the same shape as \texttt{f\_\allowbreak{}consumed} in \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}). \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}/\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} updated. \textbf{Draft v1.31, 2026-08-25 --- the recovery subsystem redesigned as a whole(direction B), and the freeze lifted.} The entire re-anchoring episode is replaced by the two-tier parent rule: (i) an unlanded parent \texttt{≤ G\_\allowbreak{}max}(deep-reorg protection unchanged); (ii) \textbf{a final L1 landed head as parent, with no gap cap(\texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max\_\allowbreak{}landed = ∞})} --- because a landed head is final, building on it reorgs no already-landed block. Recovery = a direct corollary of \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} tier (ii): during a stall, any lookahead builder(or any key holder on the forced-only path)produces one block with the landed head as its parent, L2 ordering recovers in one hop, and a stall of any duration (hours to days) is still one hop --- because tier (ii) has no cap, a recovery block never expires, and the whole family of "the proving latency punching through the freshness window" problems of the old design (r28, round 7 P1) disappears at the root. Net deletions: the announcement/\texttt{B\_\allowbreak{}ra}, the challenge/\texttt{B\_\allowbreak{}ch}/ deposit settlement, the pinned parent/re-pinning, the freshness floor/\texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}base}/\texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}ra}, the \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}cont} continuation, the episode state machine, and the two-sided \texttt{lag > Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall} condition on forced-only blocks --- and consequently open items such as r29-P2(deposit determinism)no longer apply. The argument for \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max\_\allowbreak{}landed = ∞}: the reorg exposure through a landed head is inherently capped by the length of the unlanded tail, which is in turn capped by \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} (anything beyond it is already landed and final), so "unbounded" does not increase the worst-case depth (still ≤ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}, \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}) yet buys one-hop recovery from a stall of any length; a finite value loses at both ends (below the proving latency it can never catch up with a long stall, and at \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} the tightening is an illusion). \textbf{Six rounds of the designer's own adversarial re-examination have been folded in}: ① the definition of "the current landed head" under an L1 reorg → bind it to the final depth \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} + benign stranding; ② recovery liveness = ≥1 lookahead builder returning ∨ the forced-only floor; ③ rule (ii) is not gated (gating would reopen r9-1 and is redundant --- an honest lander is inherently harmless via fork choice); ④/⑤ under a long stall, L1-sync and the forced-inclusion backlog catch up per block at \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor}/\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} (ordering in one hop, synchronization amortized); ⑥\revnote{257}the lander's tail-selection strategy = \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} fork choice + a landing depth ≥ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}prop} of propagation settling, and "the lander's view is complete" is added to the honesty clauses of \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}. Sections touched: \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} the two-tier rule, \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 2, \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} the lander's strategy, \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4} the three tiers of residual risk, \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} replaced wholesale, \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} forced-only blocks simplified, \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8} \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}anchor}, \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} the recovery row, \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} the parameters, \hyperref[app:B]{Appendix~B}. \textbf{Draft v1.30, 2026-08-25}: DeepSeek round 8 --- r31-1(Critical 1, outside the freeze): \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} still said "attach to the legal chain head with the highest slot", contradicting the "most blocks" fork choice of \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} per r11-2; it now references \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} directly (otherwise an implementation would reopen the vulnerability in which a deep skip is automatically followed). The rest: Critical 2(forced inclusion being "unconditional" versus nonce preemption)belongs to pending decision ① (r27-1 has already added the qualification); Warnings 1/2/3 all land in the frozen \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}/\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} (the "exactly covers" wording for the proving lead time versus the lookahead, the uniqueness of the first forced-only block, and the Expired transition of the episode state table) --- these will be dissolved together by the redesign of the recovery subsystem rather than patched point by point. \textbf{Draft v1.29, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 18, one P1\revnote{258}: r18-2's "batch-level unique snapshot \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}batch} = the anchor of the first block" made the forced-inclusion obligation of an already-signed block depend on the batch boundary chosen by the lander, so landing a prefix could strand a block that was otherwise legally signed. Changed to \textbf{a per-block snapshot, checked against the anchor already committed in the block header} --- the obligation is fixed at signing time and is independent of batch boundaries. (The re-anchoring parts of \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}/\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} remain frozen; this item belongs to the ordinary landing path and is outside the scope of the freeze.) \textbf{Draft v1.28, 2026-08-25}: the owner's decision --- the \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}/\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} recovery subsystem is frozen pending formalization(direction B). No more point patches to this subsystem: what the last eight rounds of review (r18/r20/r21/r23/r24/r25/r28 and round 7's P1/P2) exposed is a single family of "proving latency versus slot timing window" and deposit-settlement determinism problems, whose root-cause intersection leaves no room for a simple rule and which should be solved in one pass by a human design retrospective + state-machine model checking. A DRAFT freeze note is added at the top of \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} (listing the open items for the retrospective), and its mechanism description and numerical bounds are downgraded to provisional. Reviews landing on other parts (incentives, forced inclusion, parameters, trust-model wording, and so on) continue to be handled as usual. \textbf{Draft v1.27, 2026-08-25}: DeepSeek round 6\revnote{259}, 2 Critical + 5 warnings: Critical 2(the contradiction between \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} and \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} over the slot of the first forced-only block)was already fixed by v1.26/r25-2; r27-1 qualifies the "unconditional" claim about forced inclusion in \hyperref[sec:0]{\S0}/\hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}/\hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} in light of the nonce-preemption gap of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12}-12(Critical 1 is elevated into a correction of the wording of the claim); r27-2 specifies the different destinations of \texttt{B\_\allowbreak{}ra} under cancellation (a)/(b)/execution(under (b), when a genuine failure heals itself, the diligent announcer is refunded in full, W3); r27-3 adds the transition "the continuation state meets a normal batch" to the state table(W2); r27-4 requires \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}ra} to leave enough proving lead time, with the expected wait including one round of proving(W1); and W4/W5(the L1 cost of enqueue validation, and calibration of the \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} margin)are folded into \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} items 14/15. \textbf{Draft v1.26, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 16, two P1s(consistency propagation)\revnote{260}: r25-2, the \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} format rule for forced-only blocks still said "the first block = the execution baseline slot at window expiry", contradicting \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}/r24-1's "the \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} window at the moment of acceptance"; these are now aligned(including item 3 of the \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} fourfold division); and the Cancelled row of the state table gains r24-2's conjunct for cancellation (b), "no outstanding \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}ch}". Pure consistency, no new mechanism. \textbf{Draft v1.25, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 15, P1\revnote{261}: r24-1 makes the first forced-only block bring the head up to the wall clock, dropping \texttt{lag} to ≤ \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max}, but \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} still requires \texttt{lag > Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall} for a forced-only block, while \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}cont} is only a few L1 slots --- so a continuation batch can never wait for \texttt{lag} to exceed \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall} again, r18-3's "one window drains the backlog" fails, and a cartel can impose a stall on every batch. Fix: a continuation batch is exempted from the three valve-opening conditions of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7} and rides the forced-only authorization already established in this episode and carried by the proven chain state(strictly bound to "it extends this episode's forced-only head + \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}cont} has not elapsed empty + the queue still holds due entries", and lapsing as soon as the queue drains or the timeout hits); only the first batch still re-checks the three conditions. \textbf{Draft v1.24, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 14, P1\revnote{262}+ DeepSeek round 5, 5 warnings: r24-1 changes the reference point of the re-anchoring freshness floor from "the window-expiry \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}base}" to "the wall clock at the moment of acceptance"(proving takes about 10–15 minutes >> \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max}=64s, so by acceptance time a window-expiry reference point is already hundreds of slots stale and a single re-anchoring block still cannot catch up; binding to the moment of acceptance guarantees by construction that at the instant of landing the head is within \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} of the wall clock, which the lander implements by pre-signing and pre-proving for a near-future \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}ra})\revnote{263}; r24-2 adds the conjunct "no outstanding \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}ch}" to cancellation condition (b)(blocking a conflicting fork that excludes \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}ch} from using \texttt{lag ≤ Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} to bypass the branch commitment)\revnote{264}; r24-3 registers multiple \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}ch} and settles them independently\revnote{265}; r24-4 states explicitly that the aggregator liveness bound is conditional on permissionless fallback being economically viable\revnote{266}; r24-5 raises nonce preemption of forced entries to a blocking open item\revnote{267}. Also, as suggested, a master parameter table was added and the episode gained an Expired state. \textbf{Draft v1.23, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 13, one P1\revnote{268}: r17-1's "jump to the execution baseline slot \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}base}" was added only to forced-only re-anchoring blocks and was missing from ordinary re-anchoring blocks --- the general landing rule only rejects future slots, so colluding parties could land a stale re-anchoring block whose slot exceeds the parent's by just \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max +\allowbreak{} 1} yet lags far behind the wall clock, leaving the head still not caught up and the current round's builder unable to attach, and letting the attacker keep winning announcements with a pre-signed sparse chain and drag out recovery. Fix: both re-anchoring paths uniformly gain the freshness floor \texttt{slot ≥ s\_\allowbreak{}base}(\hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 2 + the \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} execution check), so that one re-anchoring necessarily brings the head up to the wall clock; \texttt{s\_\allowbreak{}base} is pinned by the wall clock at window expiry and does not change across re-pinning. \textbf{Draft v1.22, 2026-08-25}: DeepSeek round 4, 7 warnings + 1 suggestion(mostly consistency and wording corrections, plus one conceptual clarification): r22-1 clarifies that a "conflicting fork" does not reorg an already-landed pinned parent(the finality of \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2})but extends it and isolates the unlanded tail, and that "re-pinning" only moves the pointer forward; r22-2 aligns the definition of the re-anchoring parent in \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 2 with the pinned parent of \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}; r22-3 changes \hyperref[sec:5.1]{\S5.1} rule 4 from "all forced entries" to a reference to the \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} capacity rule of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}; r22-4 deletes the old sentence in \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4} about "capped at \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max}" that contradicted r20-1; r22-6 clarifies the exit-delay wording of \hyperref[sec:4.1]{\S4.1} (an address that exits after the snapshot still has valid lookahead slots); r22-7 states explicitly in \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} that after re-pinning a new proof must be produced against the new pinned parent; and, as suggested, an episode state-transition table was added to \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}.\revnote{269} \textbf{Draft v1.21, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 12, one P1\revnote{270}: r18-1's re-anchoring cancellation criterion, "only an extension on the same branch cancels", collapses --- \hyperref[sec:6.1]{\S6.1} requires every accepted batch to extend the pinned landed head, so every batch has the pinned parent as an ancestor and passes the "same branch" test, and a malicious deep skip cancels the re-anchoring just the same. The root cause is that the episode pinned only the parent and not the branch it was meant to protect. Changed to use an existence challenge \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}ch} as the branch commitment: cancellation happens if and only if (a) the landed chain of the accepted batch contains some challenged \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}ch}(the protected tail really did land), or (b) \texttt{lag ≤ Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}(full recovery); other batches that advance the landed head(including a conflicting fork that isolates \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}ch})only re-pin, do not cancel and preserve the window timer, while an isolated challenge is refunded \texttt{B\_\allowbreak{}ch} with neither reward nor penalty. \textbf{Draft v1.20, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 11, one P1\revnote{271}: \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max}'s per-block gap cap bounds only the rollback depth of a single block, not the depth of a branch --- \hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2} had previously claimed wrongly that it "cannot be stacked by relaying". A minority coalition can relay many blocks along a private branch(each hop's parent distance ≤ \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max}, each block legal, no equivocation), and once a colluding aggregator lands the whole branch it isolates the entire unlanded honest tail, at a depth far beyond \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max − 1}. Throughout the document (\hyperref[sec:4.2]{\S4.2}/\hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2}/\hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}/\hyperref[sec:10]{\S10}/\hyperref[sec:1]{\S1})this is changed to two honestly stated depth bounds: a single-block deep skip ≤ \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max − 1}, and a relayed sparse branch ≤ the length of the unlanded tail(≈ \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} + the fallback response, the same bound as the equivocation path but not slashable); \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} is repositioned as a knob for suppressing the feasibility of sparse branches(by raising the lookahead density the coalition needs), and the residual depth is filed under the explicit trade-off of having no protocol-level DA(\hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}/A-3). \textbf{Draft v1.19, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 10, one P1\revnote{272}: if the head of the ordinary queue is sized against the full \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}, then when every block has due bridge entries taking up to \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge}, it is permanently left with only \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force − C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} available and does not fit, so a sustained bridge flow can starve ordinary forced inclusion indefinitely --- the per-entry share of both queues is changed to "the guaranteed capacity after deducting the other queue's reserved quota"(ordinary = \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force − C\_\allowbreak{}bridge}, bridge = \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge}), so that each queue head fits into the current block under any volume of traffic on the other queue. \textbf{Draft v1.18, 2026-08-25}: the round 10 review forwarded by the owner\revnote{273}: r18-1 --- r12-1's "any accepted batch cancels it" is too broad, since a conflicting short fork from a malicious lookahead builder(no equivocation, it only has to win the landing)also cancels the re-anchoring and voids the honest first-prover's advantage, and under wall-clock timing a slow drip suffices to bypass r13-1 without falling into the censorship residual risk; cancellation is narrowed to extensions on the same branch only, a conflicting-fork reorg merely re-pins without resetting the window, and the frequency of conflicting forks is hard-constrained by the attacker's share of the lookahead(as with the deep-skip residual risk of \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}, with the proving-cost griefing recorded explicitly). r18-2 --- "the L1 height anchored by the batch" is not unique under the per-block anchor of \hyperref[sec:8]{\S8}, so it becomes a batch-level unique snapshot height \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}batch} declared explicitly by the landing transaction(= the anchor of the first block)+ a freshness cap, and the exit deadline gains a \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor\_\allowbreak{}max} staleness term. r18-3 --- if a forced-only backlog reopens a window for every batch, the cost = the number of batches × the window; a continuation episode is introduced so that only the first batch pays for a window, which matches the composite bound of \hyperref[sec:7]{\S7}. \textbf{Draft v1.17, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 9, two P1s(r17-1: after a stall with an empty backlog, the due slot of a newly submitted entry sits at the wall-clock position, so a forced-only chain crawling slot by slot at \texttt{parent.slot +\allowbreak{} 1} can never catch up with the due point and the escape hatch cannot bootstrap itself under a total cartel --- the first forced-only block of each re-anchoring episode is changed to jump to the execution baseline slot pinned by the episode, with subsequent blocks still at +1, so bootstrapping completes in one block with no discretionary time jump; r17-2: lowering \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}/\texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} can leave already-enqueued entries above the new share, and v1.16's borrowing rule does not cover the ordinary queue head --- this becomes a setter invariant: a reduction is refused unless the new share ≥ the maximum gas of that queue's unconsumed entries(implemented with a watermark), and the borrowing exception is deleted). \textbf{Draft v1.16, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 8, two P1s\revnote{274}. \textbf{Draft v1.15, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 7, one P2\revnote{275}: an off-by-one at the boundary of the fast-recovery row of \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} --- when \texttt{k} consecutive slots are missing the next block's parent distance is \texttt{k +\allowbreak{} 1}, so the fast path covers \texttt{k ≤ G\_\allowbreak{}max − 1}(= 63), and exactly 64 missing already forces the re-anchoring procedure; the table rows are redrawn along \texttt{absences ≤ G\_\allowbreak{}max − 1} / \texttt{≥ G\_\allowbreak{}max}. \textbf{Draft v1.14, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 6, two P2 consistency corrections(r14-1: the proving-failure row of \hyperref[sec:9]{\S9} had promised an exemption under which "the landing obligation is deferred" --- that exemption is the blocking open item 8 of \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} and does not yet exist, so the text is changed to state honestly the wrongful-slashing exposure of a recovery batch; r14-2: the \hyperref[app:B]{Appendix~B} entry for the re-anchoring procedure still carried the old v1.11 definition "cancelled as soon as the lag catches up", contradicting \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4}'s "any accepted batch cancels it"\revnote{276}, and has been corrected). \textbf{Draft v1.13, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 5, one P1\revnote{277}: the absolute deduplication of fallback strikes, "at most one per open-window period", can be weaponized in reverse --- a colluding fallback account lands a micro-batch after the window opens to consume that period's single strike, then keeps dripping micro-batches so that the open-window period never closes, \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg} is never filled, and the aggregator is never replaced. Strikes(and the \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg'} late count)are changed to accumulate at a rate limited by the duration of the open window: every \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike} of continuously open window in which a corresponding batch was accepted counts as one strike --- so that rate-limited anti-spam and the inevitability of accumulation under sustained dereliction hold simultaneously; the ability to continue a batch proof incrementally is listed as an engineering requirement(\hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} item 6), and exhaustion of the bond is treated as termination. \textbf{Draft v1.12, 2026-08-25}: the round 12 deep review forwarded by the owner\revnote{278}: the re-anchoring procedure becomes a pinned-parent scheme --- the announcement pins (nonce, parent hash/slot), the proof is pre-generated only against the pinned parent, and any accepted batch cancels the announcement(which dissolves the protocol-level contradiction between "pre-generating the proof" and "taking the latest parent at execution time", and at the same time seals off the path where "a partial landing fails to catch up with the threshold while the remaining tail is still cut off by the window")\revnote{279}; the existence challenge now carries a bond and is settled according to the outcome(a false challenge is no longer a free postpone button)\revnote{280}; it is stated explicitly that the re-anchoring signer is a \textbf{probabilistic bound} inside the trust model, and that under a full cartel the only certain exit is a forced-only block\revnote{281}; the deep-skip exposure gains the crucial point --- with any colluding aggregator there is no race to speak of, so it is bounded as an explicitly stated safety residual risk and the effect of fork choice is downgraded to a P2P convergence assumption rather than an L1 guarantee\revnote{282}; a fourfold division of recovery time(safety messages/queue drain/lag catch-up/discretionary service)\revnote{283}; and the leftover sentence in \hyperref[sec:5.5]{\S5.5}, "the weak-execution class contains no profitable attack", is corrected\revnote{284}. \textbf{Draft v1.11, 2026-08-25}: the round 11 deep review forwarded by the owner\revnote{285}. Two items still apply to the current version: \textbf{the deep-skip exposure\revnote{286}} --- once \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} allows the parent to point back as far as \texttt{s − G\_\allowbreak{}max}, the old coordination rule "attach to the chain head with the highest slot" would make the honest network automatically follow a deep-skip fork and isolate up to \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max − 1} honest blocks for free; \hyperref[sec:5.2]{\S5.2} is changed to prioritize "the most blocks since the fork point"(normal block production after a genuine gap is unaffected, and a deep-skip fork is downgraded to something that must win the landing race), and the exposure statements of \hyperref[sec:5.4]{\S5.4}/\hyperref[sec:10]{\S10} are correspondingly revised away from "a single slot"; \textbf{the composite time bound of the forced path under a stall\revnote{287}} is written out explicitly as max(the overdue point, the start of the stall + \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall}) + the re-anchoring window + the proving and landing latency. \textbf{Draft v1.10, 2026-08-25}: DeepSeek round 3 applied on top of v1.9(2 severe + 3 warnings + 2 suggestions)\revnote{288}: the initial value of \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}, 3 epoch, is below the upper bound of the normal lag band that the specification itself declares(19.2 < 20 minutes)and violates the r6-2 setter invariant, so it is recalibrated to 4 epoch, with \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall} moved in step to 5 epoch\revnote{289}; the formalization of \texttt{f\_\allowbreak{}consumed} is reconciled into a pair of cursors, removing the implementation ambiguity introduced by the two queues of r8-2\revnote{290}; the exit delay explicitly covers the slashing exposure of \texttt{δ\_\allowbreak{}slash} + the evidence-submission latency\revnote{291};
