the formal definition of the fixed aligned partition of lookahead windows is \texttt{W(\allowbreak{}slot) = floor(\allowbreak{}slot /\allowbreak{} 768)}\revnote{292}; and Appendix A-3 annotates the cost identity cited in \hyperref[sec:1]{\S1}\revnote{293}. \textbf{Draft v1.9, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 4, three P1s, applied on top of v1.8\revnote{294}. \textbf{Draft v1.8, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 3, two P1s, applied on top of v1.7\revnote{295}. \textbf{Draft v1.7, 2026-08-25}: the round 7 deep review forwarded by the owner, applied on top of v1.6(2 blocking + 4 high + 1 goal-level, all adopted): the lookahead seed takes a single value per window, with the consistency formula \texttt{randao\_\allowbreak{}source ≤ H\_\allowbreak{}snap ≤ the finalized L1 head < the start of the window}\revnote{296}; the forced-inclusion time bound becomes a backlog-aware formula + the bridge reserved quota \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge}\revnote{297}; the \texttt{f\_\allowbreak{}consumed} cursor gains a four-step atomic state transition\revnote{298}; the anchor is defined as a system-level implicit transaction\revnote{299}; the slot of a forced-only block is made unique as \texttt{parent.slot +\allowbreak{} 1} \revnote{300}; the late count is deduplicated per open-window period and the "zero collateral damage" claim is downgraded to an exemption mechanism(raised to a blocking open item), completed\revnote{301}; and the three-tier terminology for user promises + the acceptance-baseline note \revnote{302}. \textbf{Draft v1.6, 2026-08-25}: Codex round 2, two P1s, applied on top of v1.5\revnote{303}. \textbf{Draft v1.5, 2026-08-25}: the first draft revised through five rounds of adversarial review(one internal review)\revnote{304}. Round 5(OpenAI)fixes: a batch must not land a future slot + truncation at the lower bound of \texttt{lag}\revnote{305}; skip is corrected to the honest characterization "profitable but not slashable", with layered user semantics\revnote{306}; strikes are deduplicated per open-window period and given a \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}strike} grace period\revnote{307}; \texttt{L\_\allowbreak{}eq} gains an honest upper bound on external value plus evidence idempotence\revnote{308}; the forced-inclusion queue cursor is formalized\revnote{309}; and the trust model states explicitly that "the honest majority is an economic assumption, not a protocol-enforced one"\revnote{310}. The mechanism skeleton is complete, the parameters are initial suggested values, and \hyperref[sec:12]{\S12} lists the open items.
\end{designnote}
\clearpage\section*{Appendix E: Index of Review Notes}\label{app:E}

This appendix collects every provenance note marked \revnote{n} in the body. Each records which adversarial review round, which external reviewer, or which decision by the owner produced the rule it is attached to. The body therefore carries only normative statements; consult this appendix when tracing where a rule came from. The full per-version revision record is separate, in \hyperref[app:D]{Appendix~D}.

\item[{[103]}] \textbf{\S6.2}\quad r44, DeepSeek-on-v1.43 C1 --- splitting only the adjudicated quantity without splitting the threshold would leave the fallback window permanently open
\item[{[104]}] \textbf{\S6.2}\quad r10-2
\item[{[105]}] \textbf{\S6.2}\quad r46 recalibration: r44 once wrote 8 epoch ≈ 51.2 min, but \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov +\allowbreak{} W\_\allowbreak{}settle\_\allowbreak{}max} = 128 + 150 L1 slot = 55.6 min already exceeds it, so 8 epoch violates the very formula on this same line; once model property P9a was made non-vacuous (deployment values declared independently, inequalities cross-checked) this was caught immediately, and the value was changed to 9 epoch = 288 L1 slot, with a margin of 2 min
\item[{[106]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad r42/r44
\item[{[107]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad r44 cleanup, DeepSeek W3
\item[{[108]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad review r2-4
\item[{[109]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad introduced in r44 and numerically recalibrated in r46 --- the threshold must be raised together with the quantity it judges; for the derivation see \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2}; \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag,\allowbreak{}prov} is initially 4 epochs ≈ 25.6 minutes; the calibration of review r10-2 --- in v1.9 and earlier the initial value was 3 epochs ≈ 19.2 minutes, below the 15–20 minute upper bound of the normal lag band that \hyperref[sec:6.2]{\S6.2} itself declares, violating the setter invariant of r6-2, \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag ≥ upper bound of the normal lag band}: the window would open during normal operation and wrongly penalize an honest aggregator, so "zero false positives" would fail. After recalibration the margin is ≈ 5.6 minutes
\item[{[110]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad r42, independent review round 5, high 2 --- if the determination were made on the provisional lag, a malicious accomplice could first land a short candidate that zeroes out lag\_prov and thereby switch off fallback eligibility before an honest heavier candidate lands, cutting off its proving-fee compensation; rational fallback landers then withdraw and the bad candidate closes the window unopposed
\item[{[111]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad review r5-4 introduced the de-duplication; r13-1 corrected its granularity --- Codex round 5 pointed out that v1.12's "at most once per open-window period" is an absolute de-duplication: a fallback account colluding with the aggregator lands one deliberate micro-batch right after the window opens, absorbing that period's single strike, and thereafter drip-feeds micro-batches so that finality crawls forward arbitrarily slowly, the open-window period never closes, \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg} is never filled, and the aggregator is never replaced --- the shield against strike-spamming is turned around and used as the attacker's shield; moreover, each micro-batch that advances the landing head also invalidates the proof an honest fallback lander had pre-generated against the old chain head, so it cannot win the landing right back
\item[{[112]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad the original purpose of r5-4
\item[{[113]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad review r3-1, Codex
\item[{[114]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad review r4-2, DeepSeek --- v1.3 once specified counting per window opening, which contradicts this section's own zero-false-positive principle
\item[{[115]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad review r7-6
\item[{[116]}] \textbf{\S6.3}\quad r13-1 corrected the granularity of both counters together, away from "once per open-window period"; otherwise an aggregator riding the threshold with its own micro-batches could likewise pin \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}agg'} at 1

\item[{[127]}] \textbf{\S6.4}\quad r41 added the \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} term --- independent review round 4, medium 2: the \texttt{final\_\allowbreak{}ref} of a tier (ii) block header must be ≤ the anchor, and the anchor is at least \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} deep, so the L1 block that witnesses the parent's finality must age a further \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor} before it can be referenced; the earliest signable point = parent landing + \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1} + \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}anchor}

\item[{[139]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad the fix from review r8-2 --- under a single FIFO cursor a "reserved quota" is vacuous: a bridge entry still has to wait for every ordinary entry ahead of it to drain before its turn comes, so a paid flood can postpone it without bound
\item[{[140]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad the formalization of review r5-6
\item[{[141]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad the formalization of review r7-3; reconciled by r10-3 into a cursor pair --- after r8-2 split the queue in two, this item still spoke of a single cursor, and "each of the two cursors advances under r7-3" lacked a formal definition, so implementations would diverge on the batch start/end checks
\item[{[142]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad clarification of review r35, DeepSeek warning 3 --- the r18-2 → r30-1 material below is evolutionary history, not a second, parallel binding
\item[{[143]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad review r18-2 → r30-1 redo
\item[{[144]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad review r38, DeepSeek Critical --- the old shortcut "or the last block of the batch, whose anchor is the highest" is deleted: the anchor is only non-decreasing, so a batch may have a fresh anchor on its last block while earlier blocks carry stale anchors; checking only the last block would wave those earlier blocks through even though their forced-inclusion obligation and \texttt{m\_\allowbreak{}consumed} were computed against a stale L1 view, and forced inclusion, being the unconditionally safe path, cannot tolerate such a hole
\item[{[145]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad review r9-2, Codex --- P1
\item[{[146]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad review r16-2 introduced per-queue share validation, and r19-1 corrected the ordinary share: if bridge entries were validated against \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} alone, an entry whose gas falls in \texttt{(\allowbreak{}C\_\allowbreak{}bridge,\allowbreak{} C\_\allowbreak{}force]} could be enqueued yet never fit into the \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} bridge prefix and would deadlock at the head of the queue; and if the ordinary share were taken to be \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force}, then whenever every block has due bridge entries taking up to \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge}, an ordinary queue head that fills all of \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} would forever have only \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force − C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} available and would never fit, so a sustained bridge flow could starve ordinary forced inclusion indefinitely --- the per-entry share of both queues must therefore be taken as its "guaranteed capacity after deducting the other side's reserved quota": ordinary = \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force − C\_\allowbreak{}bridge}, bridge = \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge}, so that each queue head fits into the current block under any volume of traffic on the other side
\item[{[147]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad review r17-2 supersedes the borrowed-allowance relief rule of v1.16 --- a borrowed allowance covers only bridge entries with \texttt{≤ the C\_\allowbreak{}force share}, and cannot save an ordinary queue-head entry that exceeds the new share after \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} is lowered, so the forced-prefix obligation would make every block illegal and the deadlock would reappear
\item[{[148]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad review r24-5, DeepSeek round 5, W5
\item[{[149]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad the r37-2 correction, since the original "tier (ii) only" would be rate-limited by \texttt{F\_\allowbreak{}l1}

\item[{[152]}] \textbf{\S7}\quad r39; a lander that skips the content tail in order to land the forced-only chain is directly overridden by a content candidate within the same \hyperref[sec:5.6]{\S5.6} window, r41

\item[{[274]}] \textbf{Appendix D}\quad r16-1: the isolatable depth under equivocation collusion had been described as "hard-capped by \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}" --- opening the window is only an authorization and does not force acceptance, so it is honestly bounded as a soft upper bound of \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag} + the fallback response time, with the assumption stated explicitly; r16-2: bridge entries have their gas validated against \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}force} but can only be consumed against \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge}, so an entry in the interval \texttt{(\allowbreak{}C\_\allowbreak{}bridge,\allowbreak{} C\_\allowbreak{}force]} deadlocks the moment it reaches the queue head --- enqueue validation is changed to use the quota of the queue the entry belongs to, plus a deterministic borrow-and-drain rule for legacy oversized entries

\item[{[294]}] \textbf{Appendix D}\quad r9-1/r9-3: delete the immediate-recovery exception "if \texttt{lag > Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall} at landing time then \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} is waived" --- that condition looks only at the L1 landed head and cannot see the P2P tail, so an aggregator withholding landings + one malicious lookahead builder + a colluding lander could use it to isolate an unbounded honest tail without equivocating; and honest recovery from a genuinely large gap was forced to wait out the full \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall} --- all of it is replaced by the \hyperref[sec:6.4]{\S6.4} \textbf{re-anchoring procedure}: announcement + an existence-challenge window + cancellation as soon as the lag catches up, with the same rules applying to the landing of forced-only blocks, which also narrows the stall residual race of r2-2; r9-2: forced entries gain two deterministic lines of defence --- validation at enqueue and consume-and-discard at consumption --- sealing off "a single malformed paid entry putting the inclusion obligation and execution legality in conflict and stalling the chain permanently"
\item[{[295]}] \textbf{Appendix D}\quad r8-1: \texttt{D\_\allowbreak{}snap} gains the lookahead-horizon term, = \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}look +\allowbreak{} F\_\allowbreak{}final +\allowbreak{} margin} = 5 L1 epoch, and this is established as a setter invariant; r8-2: the forced-inclusion queue is split into ordinary/bridge dual FIFOs with dual cursors, fixing the problem that the \texttt{C\_\allowbreak{}bridge} reserved quota was purely nominal under a single FIFO
\item[{[296]}] \textbf{Appendix D}\quad r7-1, fixing the three-way contradiction around 768 slot ≈ 2 L1 epoch

\item[{[303]}] \textbf{Appendix D}\quad r6-1: the parent-gap cap \texttt{G\_\allowbreak{}max} + the stall-recovery exception, sealing off the deep-reorg path that requires no equivocation; r6-2: the on/off threshold of the forced-only valve is changed from \texttt{H\_\allowbreak{}force} to \texttt{Δ\_\allowbreak{}stall > Δ\_\allowbreak{}lag}, fixing its numerical conflict with the normal finality lag band
